    "id": "Gyes adalah salah satu dari tiga Hekatonkheir dalam mitologi Yunani, raksasa berkepala lima puluh dan bertangan seratus, putra Gaia dan Uranus.",
    "en": "Gyes is one of the three Hecatoncheires of Greek mythology, fifty-headed, hundred-handed giants born of Gaia and Uranus.",

    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Yunani, Gyes adalah salah satu raksasa mengerikan berkepala lima puluh dan bertangan seratus, putra dewi bumi Gaia dan dewa langit Uranus. Bersama saudaranya Kottos dan Briareos ia termasuk Hekatonkheir, saudara kandung para Titan dan Kiklops. Karena mereka angkuh dan buruk rupa, Uranus melemparkan mereka ke kedalaman bumi segera setelah lahir.",
      "en": "In Greek mythology Gyes is one of the terrible fifty-headed, hundred-handed giants, son of the earth goddess Gaia and the sky god Uranus. With his brothers Kottos and Briareos he is one of the Hecatoncheires, full siblings of the Titans and Cyclopes. Because they were arrogant and ugly, Uranus cast them into the depths of the earth right after birth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gyes-c01",
        "gyes-c02",
        "gyes-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kronos, yang menggulingkan Uranus, tidak membebaskan mereka karena takut akan kekuatan mereka. Zeus yang kemudian membebaskan mereka, dan sebagai balasan para Hekatonkheir membantunya melawan para Titan; setelah para Titan dilemparkan ke Tartaros, mereka menjadi penjaga penjara itu. Rupa pasti mereka tidak pernah digambarkan, baik pada zaman Antik maupun modern.",
      "en": "Kronos, who overthrew Uranus, did not free them because he feared their strength.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gyes-c01",
      "source_id": "gyes-s1",
      "quote": "Gyés (latinsky Gyes) je v řecké mytologii jeden z hrozných padesátihlavých, storukých obrů, jejichž matkou je bohyně země Gaia a otcem bůh nebe Úranos.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Yunani, Gyes adalah salah satu raksasa mengerikan berkepala lima puluh dan bertangan seratus, putra dewi bumi Gaia dan dewa langit Uranus.",
        "en": "In Greek mythology Gyes is one of the terrible fifty-headed, hundred-handed giants, whose mother is the earth goddess Gaia and whose father is the sky god Uranus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gyes-c02",
      "source_id": "gyes-s1",
      "quote": "Tři bratři Hekatoncheirové (doslova Storucí) jménem Kottos, Briareós a Gyés a jsou přímými sourozenci Titánů a Kyklopů. Byli prý zpupní a tak šerední, že se jejich otec na ně nemohl ani podívat. Proto je hned po narození svrhl do hlubin země s přísným zákazem vycházet ven.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tiga bersaudara Hekatonkheir (harfiah \"Si Bertangan Seratus\"), yaitu Kottos, Briareos, dan Gyes, adalah saudara kandung para Titan dan Kiklops; konon mereka angkuh dan begitu buruk rupa hingga ayah mereka tak sanggup memandang mereka, sehingga ia melemparkan mereka ke kedalaman bumi segera setelah lahir dengan larangan keras untuk keluar.",
        "en": "The three Hecatoncheires brothers (literally \"Hundred-Handed\"), Kottos, Briareos and Gyes, are full siblings of the Titans and Cyclopes; they were said to be arrogant and so ugly that their father could not bear to look at them, so right after birth he cast them into the depths of the earth, strictly forbidding them to come out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gyes-c03",
      "source_id": "gyes-s1",
      "quote": "Kronos to udělal a prohlásil se vládcem, ovšem Hekatoncheiry nepropustil, protože se bál jejich síly.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kronos menggulingkan ayahnya dan menyatakan diri sebagai penguasa, tetapi tidak membebaskan para Hekatonkheir karena takut akan kekuatan mereka.",
        "en": "Kronos overthrew his father and proclaimed himself ruler (vládcem), but did not free the Hecatoncheires (Hekatoncheiry), because he feared their strength (síly)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gyes-c04",
      "source_id": "gyes-s1",
      "quote": "Udělal to až Zeus, který později povstal proti vlastnímu otci Kronovi. Hekatoncheirové mu za to pomohli v boji proti Titánům a po jejich porážce a svržení do hlubin Tartaru se stali strážci jejich vězení.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Baru Zeus yang membebaskan mereka ketika ia memberontak melawan ayahnya, Kronos; sebagai balasan para Hekatonkheir membantunya melawan para Titan, dan setelah para Titan dikalahkan dan dilemparkan ke Tartaros, mereka menjadi penjaga penjara para Titan.",
        "en": "Only Zeus freed them when he rose against his father Kronos; in return the Hecatoncheires helped him fight the Titans, and after the Titans were defeated and cast into Tartarus they became the guards of their prison."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gyes-c05",
      "source_id": "gyes-s2",
      "quote": "estis en la helena mitologio unu el la teruraj kvindekkapaj, centmanaj gigantoj, kies patrino estis diino de la tero Gaja kaj patro dio de la ĉielo Urano.",
      "locator": "enkonduko",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Esperanto juga menyebut Gieso (Gyes) sebagai salah satu raksasa mengerikan berkepala lima puluh dan bertangan seratus, anak Gaia dan Uranus.",
        "en": "The Esperanto article likewise calls Gieso (Gyes) one of the terrible fifty-headed, hundred-handed giants, child of Gaia and Uranus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gyes-c06",
      "source_id": "gyes-s1",
      "quote": "O přesné podobě Hekatoncheirů nemáme jasnou představu, ani v Antice ani v moderní době nebyli nikde zobrazeni.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tidak ada gambaran jelas tentang rupa pasti para Hekatonkheir, dan mereka tidak pernah digambarkan, baik pada zaman Antik maupun zaman modern.",
        "en": "There is no clear idea of the exact appearance of the Hecatoncheires; they were never depicted, either in Antiquity or in modern times."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hongatar adalah haltija atau dewi dalam mitologi Finlandia, ibu beruang, yang menjadi tamu kehormatan dalam ritual pemakaman beruang (peijaiset).",
    "en": "Hongatar is a haltija or goddess of Finnish mythology, mother of the bear and guest of honour at the bear funeral ritual (peijaiset).",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hongatar-c01",
      "hongatar-c02",
      "hongatar-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hongatar atau Hongas adalah haltija atau dewi dalam mitologi Finlandia yang dikenal sebagai ibu beruang. Menurut kepercayaan Finlandia kuno ia adalah leluhur perempuan bangsa beruang, juga disebut Hongotar, Höngäs, Tapiotar, atau Tapiatar, dan beruang dikatakan \"keluarga Hongatar\". Namanya berasal dari \"honka\", pohon pinus.",
      "en": "Hongatar or Hongas is a haltija or goddess of Finnish mythology known as the mother of the bear. In ancient Finnish belief she is the ancestral mother of the bear kin, also called Hongotar, Höngäs, Tapiotar or Tapiatar, and bears are said to be \"of the family of Hongatar\". Her name comes from \"honka\", a pine tree.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hongatar-c01",
        "hongatar-c04",
        "hongatar-c05",
        "hongatar-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Setelah seekor beruang dibunuh, orang mengadakan peijaiset, upacara pemakaman beruang yang kadang dipentaskan seperti pernikahan, di mana tengkoraknya diangkat ke pohon pinus besar. Hongatar adalah tamu kehormatan ritual ini, dan bisa dibayangkan berwujud beruang dengan tengkorak tertua di pohon itu.",
      "en": "After a bear was killed, people held peijaiset, a funeral for the bear sometimes staged like a wedding, in which its skull was raised onto a large pine. Hongatar was the guest of honour at these rituals and could be imagined in bear form, her skull the oldest on the tree.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hongatar-c02",
        "hongatar-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Kalevala, orang tua duniawi beruang adalah Hongatar dan Ilmarinen, dan ia adalah dewi pohon pinus. Dalam puisi penggembalaan Karelia Viena ia menjadi ibu, penyusu, dan pengasuh beruang, dengan suaminya Hongonen. Wataknya muram, dingin, dan cermat, tetapi ia istri yang baik yang mendisiplinkan beruang agar tidak memangsa ternak.",
      "en": "According to the Kalevala, the bear's earthly parents are Hongatar and Ilmarinen, and she is the goddess of the pine. In White Karelian herding poems she is the bear's mother, nurse and fosterer, with her husband Hongonen. Her nature is sullen, cold and exacting, yet she is a good wife who disciplines the bears so they will not eat cattle.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hongatar-c06",
        "hongatar-c08",
        "hongatar-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Hongatar terkait erat dengan ritual peijaiset, pemakaman beruang tempat tengkorak beruang diangkat ke pohon pinus dan ia hadir sebagai tamu kehormatan.",
    "en": "Hongatar is closely tied to the peijaiset ritual, the bear funeral in which bear skulls were raised onto a pine and she was the guest of honour.",

      "source_id": "hongatar-s1",
      "quote": "After killing a bear, there was a ritual held called peijaiset, a funeral (sometimes staged like a wedding) for the bear where its skull was lifted on a large, straight pine tree (honka).",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah seekor beruang dibunuh, diadakan ritual bernama peijaiset, upacara pemakaman beruang (kadang dipentaskan seperti pernikahan) di mana tengkoraknya diangkat ke pohon pinus besar yang lurus (honka).",
        "en": "After a bear was killed, a ritual called peijaiset was held, a funeral for the bear (sometimes staged like a wedding) in which its skull was raised onto a large, straight pine (honka)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hongatar-c03",
      "source_id": "hongatar-s1",
      "quote": "Hongatar could be also imagined in bear-form, with her skull as the oldest one on the tree. She was the special, invited guest of these rituals.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hongatar juga bisa dibayangkan berwujud beruang, dengan tengkoraknya sebagai yang tertua di pohon itu; ia adalah tamu kehormatan yang diundang dalam ritual-ritual ini.",
        "en": "Hongatar could also be imagined in bear form, her skull being the oldest on the tree; she was the special invited guest of these rituals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hongatar-c04",
      "source_id": "hongatar-s1",
      "quote": "Her name comes from honka, a pine tree, the very kind of tree where bear skulls were ritualistically lifted to hang on.",
      "locator": "Name",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berasal dari \"honka\", pohon pinus, jenis pohon tempat tengkorak beruang diangkat dan digantung secara ritual.",
        "en": "Her name comes from \"honka\", a pine tree, the very kind of tree on which bear skulls were ritually hung."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hongatar-c05",
      "source_id": "hongatar-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that bears are \"of the family of Hongatar\".",
      "locator": "In runic songs",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon beruang adalah \"keluarga Hongatar\".",
        "en": "It is said that bears are \"of the family of Hongatar\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hongatar-c06",
      "source_id": "hongatar-s2",
      "quote": "Kalevalan mukaan karhun maalliset vanhemmat ovat Hongatar ja Ilmarinen. Kalevalan mukaan hän on honkapuun jumalatar.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Kalevala, orang tua duniawi beruang adalah Hongatar dan Ilmarinen, dan Hongatar adalah dewi pohon pinus.",
        "en": "According to the Kalevala, the bear's earthly parents are Hongatar and Ilmarinen, and Hongatar is the goddess of the pine tree."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hongatar-c07",
      "source_id": "hongatar-s2",
      "quote": "Hongatar, Hongotar, Hongas tai Höngäs on muinaissuomalaisen käsityksen mukaan karhusuvun emuu. Häntä kutsutaan myös rinnakkaisnimillä Tapiotar tai Tapiatar.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan Finlandia kuno, Hongatar, Hongotar, Hongas, atau Höngäs adalah leluhur perempuan (emuu) bangsa beruang; ia juga disebut Tapiotar atau Tapiatar.",
        "en": "In ancient Finnish belief Hongatar, Hongotar, Hongas or Höngäs is the ancestral mother (emuu) of the bear kin; she is also called Tapiotar or Tapiatar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hongatar-c08",
      "source_id": "hongatar-s2",
      "quote": "Vienankarjalaisissa karjanhoitorunoissa hänet on kuvattu karhun äitinä, imettäjänä ja kasvattajana. Niissä hänen puolisonsa on Hongonen, karhun isä.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam puisi penggembalaan Karelia Viena, ia digambarkan sebagai ibu, penyusu, dan pengasuh beruang, dengan suaminya Hongonen sebagai ayah beruang.",
        "en": "In White Karelian cattle-herding poems she is described as the bear's mother, nurse and fosterer, her husband Hongonen being the bear's father."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hongatar-c09",
      "source_id": "hongatar-s2",
      "quote": "Luonteeltaan häntä kuvataan juroksi, kylmäksi ja tarkaksi. Hänen kuitenkin sanotaan olevan hyvä vaimo, joka myös pitää tiukkaa kuria karhuille, etteivät ne söisi karjaa.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wataknya digambarkan muram, dingin, dan cermat, tetapi ia juga dikatakan istri yang baik yang menegakkan disiplin ketat atas beruang agar mereka tidak memangsa ternak.",
        "en": "Her character is described as sullen, cold and exacting, but she is also said to be a good wife who keeps the bears under strict discipline so they will not eat cattle."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en dan fi; rujukan Haavio (1967) dan Siikala (2012) belum dibuka langsung."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Karakura adalah roh jahat dalam mitologi Turki yang menindih orang tidur pada malam hari, mengirim mimpi buruk, dan mengganggu ibu yang baru melahirkan.",
    "en": "The karakura is a malevolent spirit of Turkish mythology that presses on sleepers at night, sends nightmares and torments new mothers.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "karakura-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Karakura, di beberapa daerah disebut Karavura, adalah roh jahat dalam mitologi Turki yang menyerang korbannya pada malam hari. Ia dipercaya terutama di Anatolia Timur dan dianggap mengirim mimpi buruk serta tindihan malam. Ia konon terutama mengganggu ibu yang baru melahirkan dan mencoba mencekik mereka saat tidur; di daerah Erzurum dan Erzincan ia disamakan dengan albastı yang mencabut paru-paru perempuan setelah melahirkan.",
      "en": "The karakura, called Karavura in some regions, is a malevolent spirit of Turkish mythology that attacks its victims at night. Believed in mainly in Eastern Anatolia, it is thought to send nightmares and night-pressings. It is said chiefly to torment new mothers, trying to strangle them in their sleep; in the Erzurum and Erzincan region it is likened to the albastı, which tears out the lungs of women after childbirth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "karakura-c01",
        "karakura-c03",
        "karakura-c04",
        "karakura-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagian legenda menggambarkannya berwujud manusia, yang lain sebagai hewan berkepala kambing sebesar kucing. Di sekitar Konya ia dianggap menindih dan mencekik orang, tetapi takut cahaya siang: saat matahari terbit ia tak bisa bergerak, dapat ditangkap, dan setelah dipaksa bersumpah bisa dijadikan budak. Anak-anak ditakut-takuti dengannya.",
      "en": "Some legends give it human form, others make it a goat-headed animal the size of a cat. Around Konya it is thought to settle on people and strangle them, but it fears daylight: at sunrise it cannot move, can be caught, and after being made to swear an oath can be used as a slave. Children are frightened with it.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "karakura-c01",
      "source_id": "karakura-s1",
      "quote": "A karakura egy ártó, gonosz lény a török mitológiában, aki vagy ami éjszaka lepi meg áldozatait. Főként Kelet-Anatóliában hisznek benne, a hiedelem szerint leginkább kismamákat zaklat, megpróbálja őket álmukban megfojtani.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karakura adalah makhluk jahat dalam mitologi Turki yang menyerang korbannya pada malam hari; terutama dipercaya di Anatolia Timur, ia konon terutama mengganggu ibu yang baru melahirkan dan mencoba mencekik mereka saat tidur.",
        "en": "The karakura is a harmful, evil being of Turkish mythology that surprises its victims at night; believed in mainly in Eastern Anatolia, it is said chiefly to torment new mothers, trying to strangle them in their sleep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karakura-c02",
      "source_id": "karakura-s1",
      "quote": "Más területek hitvilága szerint kivágja és ellopja áldozatai tüdejét. Egyes mondák szerint emberalakban, más mondák szerint kecskefejű, macska nagyságú állat képében jelenik meg. Vele ijesztgetik a gyerekeket.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan di daerah lain, ia memotong dan mencuri paru-paru korbannya; sebagian legenda menyebut ia muncul berwujud manusia, yang lain sebagai hewan berkepala kambing sebesar kucing; anak-anak ditakut-takuti dengannya.",
        "en": "In the beliefs of other areas it cuts out and steals its victims' lungs; some legends say it appears in human form, others as a goat-headed animal the size of a cat; children are frightened with it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karakura-c03",
      "source_id": "karakura-s2",
      "quote": "Karakura (bazı yörelerde Karavura); acıklı, kötücül bir ruhun adı.",
      "locator": "giriş",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karakura (di beberapa daerah Karavura) adalah nama roh jahat yang menyedihkan.",
        "en": "Karakura (in some regions Karavura) is the name of a woeful, malevolent spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karakura-c04",
      "source_id": "karakura-s2",
      "quote": "İnsanlara korkulu kâbusları ve karabasanları gönderen odur.",
      "locator": "giriş",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dialah yang mengirim mimpi buruk yang menakutkan dan tindihan malam (karabasan) kepada manusia.",
        "en": "It is the one who sends people frightening nightmares and night-pressings (karabasan)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karakura-c05",
      "source_id": "karakura-s2",
      "quote": "Erzurum ve Erzincan yöresindeki inanışlara göre bu tabiat üstü güç, albastı gibi lohusalara musallat olan, onları korkutarak, ciğerlerini söküp götüren bir varlıktır.",
      "locator": "giriş",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan di daerah Erzurum dan Erzincan, kekuatan gaib ini, seperti albastı, mengganggu perempuan yang baru melahirkan, menakut-nakuti mereka, dan mencabut serta membawa pergi paru-paru mereka.",
        "en": "In the beliefs of the Erzurum and Erzincan region this supernatural force, like the albastı, haunts women after childbirth, frightening them and tearing out and carrying off their lungs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karakura-c06",
      "source_id": "karakura-s2",
      "quote": "Konya civarında anlatıldığına göre, bu ruh, keçiye benzeyen fakat kedi büyüklüğünde olan, insanların üstüne çökerek onları boğmaya çalışan bir yaratık şeklinde düşünülür. Gün ışığından korkar, güneş doğunca kımıldayamaz; ancak o zaman yakalanabilir. Ona yemin ettirdikten sonra köle gibi kullanmak mümkün olurmuş.",
      "locator": "giriş",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di sekitar Konya, roh ini dibayangkan sebagai makhluk mirip kambing tetapi sebesar kucing yang menindih orang dan mencoba mencekik mereka; ia takut cahaya siang dan tak bisa bergerak saat matahari terbit, sehingga hanya saat itu bisa ditangkap, dan setelah dipaksa bersumpah ia bisa dijadikan budak.",
        "en": "Around Konya this spirit is imagined as a goat-like creature the size of a cat that settles on people and tries to strangle them; it fears daylight and cannot move once the sun rises, when alone it can be caught, and after being made to swear an oath it can be used like a slave."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kitovras adalah makhluk setengah manusia setengah kuda dalam apokrif Rusia, saudara sekaligus saingan Raja Salomo yang terkenal karena kebijaksanaannya.",
    "en": "Kitovras is a half-man, half-horse being of Russian apocrypha, brother and rival of King Solomon, famed for his wisdom.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kitovras-c01",
      "kitovras-c03",
      "kitovras-c04",
      "kitovras-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kitovras adalah makhluk setengah manusia setengah kuda, tokoh dalam kisah-kisah apokrif. Namanya bisa berarti monster secara umum atau nama diri, dan ia digambarkan sebagai monster kentaur, kadang bersayap. Raja Salomo dan Kitovras sudah digambarkan pada panel Pintu Vasilievsky (1335–1336) di Katedral Santa Sofia, Novgorod.",
      "en": "Kitovras is a half-man, half-horse being, a hero of apocryphal tales. The name could mean a monster in general or be a proper name, and he was depicted as a centaur-like monster, sometimes winged. King Solomon and Kitovras already appear on a panel of the Vasilievsky Gates (1335–1336) of St Sophia's Cathedral in Novgorod.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kitovras-c01",
        "kitovras-c02",
        "kitovras-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah-kisah tentang Salomo, Kitovras kadang menjadi saudara dan rekannya, kadang musuh dan saingannya. Ia makhluk yang tahu masa depan: ketika ditangkap Salomo, ia membuatnya takjub dengan kebijaksanaannya. Dalam kisah kemudian ia memerintah kota Lukorye sebagai raja manusia pada siang hari dan raja hewan pada malam hari, dan kadang menculik istri Salomo lalu tewas bertarung dengannya.",
      "en": "In the tales of Solomon, Kitovras is sometimes his brother and companion, sometimes his enemy and rival. He is a prophetic being: captured by Solomon, he astonishes him with his wisdom. In later tales he rules the city of Lukorye, king of men by day and of beasts by night, and sometimes abducts Solomon's wife and dies fighting him.",

      "source_id": "kitovras-s1",
      "quote": "Получеловек-полуконь, герой апокрифических сказаний",
      "locator": "карточка",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitovras adalah makhluk setengah manusia setengah kuda, tokoh dalam kisah-kisah apokrif.",
        "en": "Kitovras is a half-man, half-horse being, a hero of apocryphal tales."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kitovras-c02",
      "source_id": "kitovras-s1",
      "quote": "Под Китоврасом подразумевалось вообще чудовище, или имя собственное. Изображался как чудовище-кентавр, иногда с крыльями.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitovras bisa berarti monster secara umum atau nama diri; ia digambarkan sebagai monster kentaur, kadang bersayap.",
        "en": "Kitovras could mean a monster in general or a proper name; it was depicted as a centaur-like monster, sometimes winged."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kitovras-c03",
      "source_id": "kitovras-s1",
      "quote": "В разных сказаниях о Соломоне Китоврас выступает то как его брат и сподвижник, то как враг и соперник.",
      "locator": "Апокрифическое сказание",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam berbagai kisah tentang Salomo, Kitovras tampil kadang sebagai saudara dan rekannya, kadang sebagai musuh dan saingannya.",
        "en": "In various tales of Solomon, Kitovras appears now as his brother and companion, now as his enemy and rival."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kitovras-c04",
      "source_id": "kitovras-s1",
      "quote": "Китоврас является существом вещим: изловленный царём Соломоном, он удивляет его своей мудростью. В позднейших сказаниях Китоврас царствует «в граде Лукорье», где днём он царь над людьми, ночью — над зверями. Он иногда является похитителем жены у Соломона и гибнет в бою с ним.",
      "locator": "Апокрифическое сказание",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitovras adalah makhluk yang tahu masa depan: ketika ditangkap Raja Salomo, ia membuatnya takjub dengan kebijaksanaannya; dalam kisah-kisah kemudian ia memerintah \"di kota Lukorye\", pada siang hari sebagai raja manusia dan pada malam hari sebagai raja hewan, dan kadang ia menculik istri Salomo lalu tewas dalam pertarungan dengannya.",
        "en": "Kitovras is a prophetic being: captured by King Solomon, he astonishes him with his wisdom; in later tales Kitovras reigns \"in the city of Lukorye\", king of men by day and of beasts by night, and sometimes he abducts Solomon's wife and dies fighting him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kitovras-c05",
      "source_id": "kitovras-s1",
      "quote": "Царь Соломон и Китоврас. Клеймо Васильевских врат. 1335—1336 гг. Софийского собора в Новгороде.",
      "locator": "иллюстрация",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raja Salomo dan Kitovras digambarkan pada panel Pintu Vasilievsky (1335–1336) di Katedral Santa Sofia, Novgorod.",
        "en": "King Solomon and Kitovras are depicted on a panel of the Vasilievsky Gates (1335–1336) of the Cathedral of St Sophia in Novgorod."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kitovras-c06",
      "source_id": "kitovras-s1",
      "quote": "мифическое существо, упоминаемое в русских апокрифах",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitovras adalah makhluk mitos yang disebut dalam apokrif Rusia.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lakanica adalah sejenis makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat tradisional Polandia, yaitu roh ladang atau padang rumput. Mereka dikenal pemalu dan sulit ditemui, dan dapat muncul dalam wujud perempuan. Peneliti Alanna Muniz mencatat bahwa lakanica mirip jenis vila atau rusalka lainnya dalam cerita budaya Slavia Barat. Lakanica juga muncul dalam novel abad ke-21 The Dollmaker of Kraków.",
      "en": "The lakanica is a kind of supernatural being of traditional Polish folklore, a spirit of fields or meadows. They are reputed to be shy and elusive and can appear in female form. The scholar Alanna Muniz notes that the lakanica resembles other kinds of vily or rusalki in the stories of West Slavic cultures. A lakanica also appears in the 21st-century novel The Dollmaker of Kraków.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Leiger adalah raksasa dalam cerita rakyat Estonia, pahlawan utama legenda Pulau Hiiumaa dan kerabat sekaligus saingan Suur Tõll dari Saaremaa.",
    "en": "Leiger is a giant of Estonian folklore, main hero of the legends of Hiiumaa island and kinsman and rival of Suur Tõll of Saaremaa.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "leiger-c01",
      "leiger-c02",
      "leiger-c07"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Leiger adalah tokoh mitis dalam folklor Estonia, seorang pahlawan perkasa atau raksasa, dan tokoh utama legenda Pulau Hiiumaa, sebagaimana Suur Tõll menjadi tokoh utama di Saaremaa. Dalam sebagian kisah ia keponakan Tõll, dalam kisah lain saudaranya, dan hampir sama kuatnya. Keduanya bersahabat tetapi kadang bertengkar karena Tõll ingin selalu berkuasa.",
      "en": "Leiger is a mythical figure of Estonian folklore, a strongman or giant, and the main character of the legends of Hiiumaa island, as Suur Tõll is on Saaremaa. In some tales he is Tõll's nephew, in others his brother, and almost as mighty. The two were friends but sometimes quarrelled because Tõll always wanted to be in charge.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "leiger-c01",
        "leiger-c02",
        "leiger-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda, tanjung pasir Kassari adalah jalan ke Saaremaa yang tak selesai dibangun Leiger dan putra-putranya. Dalam satu kisah ia menyeberangi laut sedalam empat depa dari Saaremaa ke Hiiumaa dengan berjalan kaki; dalam kisah lain Tõll yang menginap melarikan diri karena takut mendengar Leiger berbalik di tempat tidur hingga jendela bergetar. Makamnya yang panjangnya tujuh depa konon ada di desa Kõpu.",
      "en": "Legend says the Kassari spit is the road to Saaremaa left unfinished by Leiger and his sons. In one tale he walked across the four-fathom-deep sea from Saaremaa to Hiiumaa; in another, Tõll, staying the night, fled in fright when he heard Leiger turn over in bed so that the windows rattled. His seven-fathom-long grave is said to lie in the village of Kõpu.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "leiger-c03",
        "leiger-c04",
        "leiger-c05",
        "leiger-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada 1997 sebuah patung perunggu Leiger setinggi 3,7 meter karya Kalju Reitel didirikan di Kassari, dan sejak 2016 sebuah kapal feri bernama Leiger melayani lintasan Rohuküla–Heltermaa.",
      "en": "In 1997 a 3.7-metre bronze statue of Leiger by Kalju Reitel was erected in Kassari, and since 2016 a ferry named Leiger has served the Rohuküla–Heltermaa route.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "leiger-c08",
        "leiger-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "title": "Leiger",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "leiger-c01",
      "source_id": "leiger-s1",
      "quote": "— герой эстонской мифологии и эстонского фольклора, в легендах — главный герой острова Хийумаа, похожий на Большого Тылля с острова Сааремаа.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Leiger adalah tokoh mitologi dan cerita rakyat Estonia, pahlawan utama legenda Pulau Hiiumaa, mirip Suur Tõll (Tõll Agung) dari Pulau Saaremaa.",
        "en": "Leiger is a hero of Estonian mythology and folklore, the main hero of the legends of Hiiumaa island, similar to Big Tõll (Suur Tõll) of Saaremaa island."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "leiger-c02",
      "source_id": "leiger-s1",
      "quote": "В одних сказаниях — племянник Большого Тылля, в других — его брат, и почти такой же могучий великан. Лейгер и Суур Тылль дружили, но иногда и ссорились, так как Тылль во всём хотел главенствовать.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian kisah ia keponakan Tõll Agung, dalam kisah lain saudaranya, dan raksasa yang hampir sama kuatnya; Leiger dan Suur Tõll bersahabat tetapi kadang bertengkar karena Tõll selalu ingin berkuasa.",
        "en": "In some tales he is Big Tõll's nephew, in others his brother, and a giant almost as mighty; Leiger and Suur Tõll were friends but sometimes quarrelled, since Tõll always wanted to be in charge."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "leiger-c03",
      "source_id": "leiger-s1",
      "quote": "Легенда гласит, что коса Кассари, которая тянется в море с Хийумаа — это недостроенная великаном Лейгером и его сыновьями дорога на Сааремаа, к Тыллю.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, tanjung pasir Kassari yang menjorok ke laut dari Hiiumaa adalah jalan ke Saaremaa menuju Tõll yang tak selesai dibangun oleh raksasa Leiger dan putra-putranya.",
        "en": "Legend says the Kassari spit stretching into the sea from Hiiumaa is the road to Saaremaa, to Tõll, left unfinished by the giant Leiger and his sons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "leiger-c04",
      "source_id": "leiger-s1",
      "quote": "Согласно народным сказаниям, могила Лейгера длиной семь саженей и глубиной две сажени находится на территории посёлка Кыпу.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat, makam Leiger yang panjangnya tujuh depa dan dalamnya dua depa terletak di wilayah desa Kõpu.",
        "en": "According to folk tales, Leiger's grave, seven fathoms long and two fathoms deep, lies in the village of Kõpu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "leiger-c05",
      "source_id": "leiger-s1",
      "quote": "История первая. Лейгер решил дойти пешком с Сааремаа на Хийумаа. Глубина моря составляла четыре сажени, но Лейгер всё же прошёл.",
      "locator": "Примеры народных сказаний",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu kisah, Leiger memutuskan berjalan kaki dari Saaremaa ke Hiiumaa; laut sedalam empat depa, tetapi Leiger tetap menyeberanginya.",
        "en": "In one tale Leiger decided to walk from Saaremaa to Hiiumaa; the sea was four fathoms deep, but Leiger crossed it anyway."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "leiger-c06",
      "source_id": "leiger-s1",
      "quote": "Ночью Большой Тылль слышит, что Лейгер в постели поворачивается так, что окна дребезжат. Испугался Тылль (хотя он был сильнее Лейгера) и убежал.",
      "locator": "Примеры народных сказаний",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah lain, ketika Tõll Agung menginap untuk beradu kekuatan, pada malam hari ia mendengar Leiger berbalik di tempat tidur hingga jendela bergetar, lalu Tõll ketakutan (meski ia lebih kuat dari Leiger) dan melarikan diri.",
        "en": "In another tale, when Big Tõll stayed over to test their strength, at night he heard Leiger turn over in bed so that the windows rattled; Tõll took fright (though he was stronger than Leiger) and ran away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "leiger-c07",
      "source_id": "leiger-s2",
      "quote": "Leiger on eesti rahvaluulest tuntud müütiline tegelane (vägilane ehk hiid). Leiger on hiiumuistendite peategelane Hiiumaa rahvaluules (Saaremaal on peategelane Suur Tõll).",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Leiger adalah tokoh mitis yang dikenal dari folklor Estonia (seorang pahlawan perkasa atau raksasa), tokoh utama legenda Hiiumaa dalam folklor pulau itu, sementara di Saaremaa tokoh utamanya Suur Tõll.",
        "en": "Leiger is a mythical figure known from Estonian folklore (a strongman or giant), the main character of Hiiumaa legends, whereas on Saaremaa the main character is Suur Tõll."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "leiger-c08",
      "source_id": "leiger-s2",
      "quote": "1997. aastal püstitati Kassarisse Sääretirbi teeristi 3,7 meetri kõrgune pronksist Leigri kuju, mille autor on skulptor Kalju Reitel.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1997 sebuah patung perunggu Leiger setinggi 3,7 meter karya pematung Kalju Reitel didirikan di persimpangan Sääretirp, Kassari.",
        "en": "In 1997 a 3.7-metre bronze statue of Leiger by the sculptor Kalju Reitel was erected at the Sääretirp crossroads in Kassari."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "leiger-c09",
      "source_id": "leiger-s2",
      "quote": "2016. aastal alustas Rohuküla-Heltermaa liini teenindamist parvlaev Leiger.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak 2016 kapal feri Leiger melayani lintasan Rohuküla–Heltermaa.",
        "en": "In 2016 the ferry Leiger began serving the Rohuküla–Heltermaa route."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Linchetto adalah folletto (peri kecil usil) dalam tradisi rakyat Provinsi Lucca yang mengganggu tidur orang pada malam hari dengan duduk di dada mereka.",
    "en": "The linchetto is a mischievous sprite (folletto) of the folk tradition of the province of Lucca that disturbs people's sleep at night by sitting on their chests.",
    "claim_ids": [

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Linchetto adalah folletto dalam tradisi rakyat Provinsi Lucca, yakni di Lucchesia, Versilia, dan Garfagnana. Menurut Vocabolario lucchese karya Idelfonso Nieri, ia roh yang tidak jahat tetapi usil, yang masuk ke kamar pada malam hari, menyingkap selimut, mengacak barang, dan terkekeh atas leluconnya. Dalam cerita rakyat Italia, linchetti duduk di dada orang tidur, sehingga orang tua bisa mati dan orang muda bermimpi buruk.",
      "en": "The linchetto is a folletto of the folk tradition of the province of Lucca, in Lucchesia, Versilia and Garfagnana. According to Idelfonso Nieri's Vocabolario lucchese it is a spirit not wicked but mischievous, which enters bedrooms at night, uncovers people, upsets things and sniggers at its pranks. In Italian folklore the linchetti sit on sleepers' chests, so that the old may die and the young have nightmares.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "linchetto-c01",
        "linchetto-c05",
        "linchetto-c06"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Linchetti digambarkan bertubuh pendek, sekitar 80 cm, dengan telinga panjang; dalam kesaksian lain ia berwujud mirip anjing, kucing, burung, atau campuran tikus, burung, dan manusia. Dalam kebanyakan cerita ia lebih jahat daripada buffardello, bahkan disamakan dengan setan. Ia masuk ke kandang, menyayangi hewan tertentu dan menyiksa yang lain.",
      "en": "The linchetti are pictured as short, about 80 cm, with long ears; in other testimonies it resembles a dog, cat or bird, or a hybrid of mouse, bird and man. In most tales it is more malign than the buffardello, even likened to the devil. It gets into stables, favouring some animals and tormenting others.",

      "en": "Its name supposedly derives from Latin incubus, and according to Felice Del Beccaro it is a relic of the woodland god Faunus in his Incubus aspect. One remedy is to put a pan of grain on the sleeper's chest, so the linchetto busies itself gathering the spilt grain until it tires.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c01",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s1",
      "quote": "(ед. число — Линкетто, итал. Linchetto) — в итальянском фольклоре существа, которые мешают людям спать по ночам. Согласно представлениям, линкетти садятся спящим на грудь, отчего старики могут даже умереть, а молодым снятся кошмары.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Italia, linchetti (tunggal: linchetto) adalah makhluk yang mengganggu tidur orang pada malam hari; mereka konon duduk di dada orang tidur, sehingga orang tua bisa sampai mati dan orang muda bermimpi buruk.",
        "en": "In Italian folklore the linchetti (singular linchetto) are beings that disturb people's sleep at night; they are believed to sit on sleepers' chests, so that old people may even die and young people have nightmares."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c02",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s1",
      "quote": "Линкетти невысоки: их рост около 80 см. Постоянной чертой их облика являются длинные уши, в остальном он может меняться.",
      "locator": "Происхождение персонажа",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Linchetti bertubuh pendek, sekitar 80 cm, dengan telinga panjang sebagai ciri tetap, sedangkan bagian lain rupanya bisa berubah.",
        "en": "The linchetti are short, about 80 cm tall; long ears are their constant feature, while the rest of their appearance may vary."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c03",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s1",
      "quote": "Первый способ — положить на грудь спящего сковороду с зерном. Забираясь на грудь спящего, линкетто рассыплет зерно. Тогда страсть к порядку заставит его собирать зёрна, и он утомится.",
      "locator": "Вред от линкетти и борьба с ними",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu cara mengatasinya adalah menaruh wajan berisi biji-bijian di dada orang tidur: linchetto yang naik akan menumpahkan biji itu, lalu kegemarannya akan keteraturan memaksanya memunguti biji-biji itu hingga kelelahan.",
        "en": "One remedy is to put a pan of grain on the sleeper's chest: climbing up, the linchetto spills the grain, and its passion for order makes it gather the grains until it is exhausted."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c04",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s1",
      "quote": "Считается, что линкетти могут забираться в стойла, где подкармливают любимых животных и морят нелюбимых. Согласно поверьям, если расплести гриву лошади, заплетённую линкетти, лошадь заболеет и умрёт.",
      "locator": "Вред от линкетти и борьба с ними",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Linchetti dipercaya bisa masuk ke kandang, memberi makan hewan kesayangan mereka dan menyiksa hewan yang tidak mereka sukai; bila surai kuda yang dikepang linchetti diurai, kuda itu konon akan sakit dan mati.",
        "en": "Linchetti are believed to get into stables, feeding the animals they like and starving those they dislike; if a horse's mane plaited by linchetti is unbraided, the horse will sicken and die."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c05",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s2",
      "quote": "Il linchetto è un folletto presente nella tradizione popolare della provincia di Lucca, sia in Lucchesia, che in Versilia, che in Garfagnana.",
      "locator": "introduzione",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Linchetto adalah folletto (peri kecil usil) dalam tradisi rakyat Provinsi Lucca, baik di Lucchesia, Versilia, maupun Garfagnana.",
        "en": "The linchetto is a folletto (sprite) of the folk tradition of the province of Lucca, in Lucchesia, Versilia and Garfagnana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c06",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s2",
      "quote": "Secondo il Vocabolario lucchese di Idelfonso Nieri, il linchetto è uno spirito non cattivo ma dispettoso, che va di notte, entra per le camere, scopre le persone, sconvolge, tramuta gli oggetti che ci sono e sghignazza delle burle che fa.",
      "locator": "introduzione",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Vocabolario lucchese karya Idelfonso Nieri, linchetto adalah roh yang tidak jahat tetapi usil: ia berkeliaran pada malam hari, masuk ke kamar, menyingkap selimut orang, mengacak-acak dan memindahkan barang, lalu terkekeh atas lelucon yang ia buat.",
        "en": "According to Idelfonso Nieri's Vocabolario lucchese, the linchetto is a spirit not wicked but mischievous, which goes about at night, enters bedrooms, uncovers people, upsets and moves the objects there, and sniggers at the pranks it plays."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c07",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s2",
      "quote": "Il nome deriverebbe dal latino incubus con agglutinazione dell'articolo, attraverso le forme lincubetto, lincuetto",
      "locator": "introduzione",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya diduga berasal dari kata Latin incubus dengan penggabungan kata sandang, melalui bentuk lincubetto dan lincuetto.",
        "en": "The name supposedly derives from Latin incubus with the article fused on, through the forms lincubetto and lincuetto."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c08",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s2",
      "quote": "Secondo l'italianista Felice Del Beccaro, infatti, il linchetto sarebbe un relitto del dio silvestre Fauno che nel suo aspetto di Incubus spaventava la gente e tormentava gli uomini con sogni cattivi e apparizioni",
      "locator": "introduzione",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut pakar sastra Italia Felice Del Beccaro, linchetto adalah sisa dari dewa hutan Faunus, yang dalam wujudnya sebagai Incubus menakuti orang dan menyiksa manusia dengan mimpi buruk dan penampakan.",
        "en": "According to the Italianist Felice Del Beccaro, the linchetto is a relic of the woodland god Faunus, who in his aspect as Incubus frightened people and tormented them with bad dreams and apparitions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c09",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s2",
      "quote": "In alcuni casi il linchetto viene identificato con il buffardello, tuttavia nella maggior parte dei racconti e delle testimonianze sembra emergere un carattere più maligno e pericoloso del linchetto rispetto all'altro, tanto che il linchetto viene anche assimilato al diavolo o comunque a uno spirito malvagio.",
      "locator": "introduzione",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang linchetto disamakan dengan buffardello, tetapi dalam kebanyakan cerita dan kesaksian ia tampak lebih jahat dan berbahaya, sampai disamakan dengan setan atau roh jahat.",
        "en": "Sometimes the linchetto is identified with the buffardello, but in most tales and testimonies it appears more malign and dangerous, so that it is even likened to the devil or an evil spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "linchetto-c10",
      "source_id": "linchetto-s2",
      "quote": "si pensa che sia un animale simile al cane o al gatto oppure un uccello, o che sia un ibrido di specie diverse (topo, uccello e uomo).",
      "locator": "introduzione",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dibayangkan sebagai hewan mirip anjing atau kucing, atau burung, atau campuran berbagai spesies (tikus, burung, dan manusia).",
        "en": "It is thought to be an animal like a dog or cat, or a bird, or a hybrid of different species (mouse, bird and man)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Watak linchetto",
        "en": "Nature of the linchetto"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Roh yang tidak jahat tetapi usil (Nieri).",
            "en": "A spirit not wicked but mischievous (Nieri)."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "linchetto-c06"
          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Lebih jahat dan berbahaya, disamakan dengan setan.",
            "en": "More malign and dangerous, likened to the devil."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "linchetto-c09"
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lugat adalah makhluk mirip vampir dalam mitologi Albania, mayat hidup yang berdiam di kegelapan, bisa terbang, dan menyerang korbannya saat tidur.",
    "en": "The lugat is a vampire-like being of Albanian mythology, an undead that dwells in darkness, can fly and attacks its victims in their sleep.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lugat-c01",
      "lugat-c02",
      "lugat-c03",
      "lugat-c04"

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lugat atau liogat adalah makhluk mirip vampir dalam mitologi Albania. Arshi Pipa menggambarkannya sebagai jiwa tersiksa yang dimuntahkan bumi karena suatu kejahatan berat. Lugat berdiam di bayang-bayang dan tempat yang tak pernah terkena sinar matahari, seperti sumur, reruntuhan tua, dan gua; rupanya menakutkan dan ia sangat ganas.",
      "en": "The lugat or liogat is a vampire-like being of Albanian mythology. Arshi Pipa described it as a tormented soul cast out by the earth for some grave wrongdoing. Lugats dwell in shadows and places never touched by sunlight, such as wells, old ruins and caves; they look frightening and are extremely violent.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lugat-c01",
        "lugat-c02",
        "lugat-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Lugat bisa terbang dan menunggang angin, menyerang korban saat tidur, dan memikat orang, terutama anak-anak, dari kegelapan. Mereka sering dipercaya mengenakan kulit orang mati dan berkuku panjang. Sebagai mayat hidup mereka tak bisa dibunuh pada malam hari, tetapi bisa dibakar bila tertangkap di makamnya.",
      "en": "A lugat can fly and ride the winds, attacks victims in their sleep and lures people, especially children, out of the dark. They are often believed to wear a dead person's skin and have long fingernails. As undead they cannot be killed at night, but can be burnt if caught in their graves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lugat-c03",
        "lugat-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Kosovo dipercaya bahwa orang mati bisa bangkit sebagai lugat bila tidak diletakkan besi di dekatnya, karena besi menjauhkan roh. Menurut Hahn, orang utara percaya orang Kristen tak pernah menjadi lugat, sedangkan di selatan tidak ada pembedaan agama. Nama lugat mungkin berasal dari Latin Vulgar *lupus peccatus, dan kata llugat juga dipakai untuk orang jahat.",
      "en": "In Kosovo it was believed that the dead could rise as lugats if no iron was placed beside them, since iron keeps spirits away. According to Hahn, northerners held that Christians never became lugats, whereas in the south there was no religious distinction. The name may come from Vulgar Latin *lupus peccatus, and the word llugat is also used for a wicked person.",
      "claim_ids": [

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "lugat-c01",
      "source_id": "lugat-s1",
      "quote": "A lugat or liogat is a vampire-like being in Albanian mythology. According to Orel the etymology \"Lugat\" is borrowed from Vulgar Latin *lupus peccatus (\"false wolf\"), though with difficult semantics.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lugat atau liogat adalah makhluk mirip vampir dalam mitologi Albania; menurut Orel, kata \"lugat\" dipinjam dari Latin Vulgar *lupus peccatus (\"serigala palsu\"), meski maknanya sulit dijelaskan.",
        "en": "A lugat or liogat is a vampire-like being in Albanian mythology; according to Orel the word \"lugat\" is borrowed from Vulgar Latin *lupus peccatus (\"false wolf\"), though the semantics are difficult."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lugat-c02",
      "source_id": "lugat-s1",
      "quote": "They abide in shadows and darkness, especially places that never see sunlight, such as inside water wells, old ruins, and caves. They have a frightening visage and are extremely violent.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka berdiam di bayang-bayang dan kegelapan, terutama tempat yang tak pernah terkena sinar matahari seperti dalam sumur, reruntuhan tua, dan gua; rupa mereka menakutkan dan mereka sangat ganas.",
        "en": "They dwell in shadows and darkness, especially places never touched by sunlight, such as wells, old ruins and caves; they have a frightening face and are extremely violent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lugat-c03",
      "source_id": "lugat-s1",
      "quote": "A lugat can fly and ride the winds, and assails his victims in their sleep. He also lures people, especially children, to himself while he is concealed in darkness.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lugat bisa terbang dan menunggang angin, menyerang korbannya saat tidur, dan memikat orang, terutama anak-anak, sambil bersembunyi dalam gelap.",
        "en": "A lugat can fly and ride the winds, attacks its victims in their sleep, and lures people, especially children, while hidden in darkness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lugat-c04",
      "source_id": "lugat-s1",
      "quote": "As undead they can't be killed during night but if they are caught in their graves, they can be burnt to death. Lugats are often believed to wear the skin of a dead person and have long fingernails.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai mayat hidup mereka tak bisa dibunuh pada malam hari, tetapi bila tertangkap di makamnya mereka bisa dibakar sampai mati; lugat sering dipercaya mengenakan kulit orang mati dan berkuku panjang.",
        "en": "As undead they cannot be killed at night, but if caught in their graves they can be burnt to death; lugats are often believed to wear a dead person's skin and to have long fingernails."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lugat-c05",
      "source_id": "lugat-s1",
      "quote": "In Albanian, the word llugat is also used to describe a wicked or frightening person.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Albania, kata llugat juga dipakai untuk menyebut orang yang jahat atau menakutkan.",
        "en": "In Albanian the word llugat is also used for a wicked or frightening person."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lugat-c06",
      "source_id": "lugat-s2",
      "quote": "Lugati është një figurë e mitologjisë shqiptare, e përshkruar nga Arshi Pipa si \"shpirt i munduar që toka e flak jashtë për shkak të ndonjë keqbërjeje të rëndë\".",
      "locator": "hyrje",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lugat adalah tokoh mitologi Albania yang digambarkan Arshi Pipa sebagai \"jiwa tersiksa yang dimuntahkan bumi karena suatu kejahatan berat\".",
        "en": "The lugat is a figure of Albanian mythology described by Arshi Pipa as \"a tormented soul that the earth casts out because of some grave wrongdoing\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lugat-c07",
      "source_id": "lugat-s2",
      "quote": "Në Kosovë besohet se lugat ngrihej edhe i vdekuri që nuk i është vënë pranë fillorja e pushkës, masha, drapëri ose ndonjë hekur tjetër sepse, me sa duket, hekuri i mban larg të gjithë shpirtërat.",
      "locator": "Besimet",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Kosovo dipercaya bahwa orang mati juga bisa bangkit sebagai lugat bila di dekatnya tidak diletakkan laras senapan, penjepit api, sabit, atau besi lain, karena besi dianggap menjauhkan semua roh.",
        "en": "In Kosovo it is believed that a dead person also rose as a lugat if no gun barrel, fire tongs, sickle or other iron was placed beside them, since iron apparently keeps all spirits away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lugat-c08",
      "source_id": "lugat-s2",
      "quote": "Hahn shton se për veriorët me të cilët kishte rënë në kontakt, të krishterët nuk çoheshin kurrë lugat, ndërsa ndër jugorë nuk kishte dallim fetar në të qenët lugat.",
      "locator": "Besimet",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hahn menambahkan bahwa menurut orang utara yang ia temui, orang Kristen tidak pernah bangkit menjadi lugat, sedangkan di kalangan orang selatan tidak ada pembedaan agama dalam menjadi lugat.",
        "en": "Hahn adds that among the northerners he met, Christians never rose as lugats, whereas among southerners there was no religious distinction in becoming a lugat."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Maruda adalah demon perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia yang menyiksa bayi di buaian dan membuat mereka tak bisa tidur.",
    "en": "Maruda is a female demon of Slavic mythology who torments babies in their cradles and keeps them from sleeping.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "maruda-slavic-demon-c01",
      "maruda-slavic-demon-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Maruda adalah demon berwujud perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia yang menyiksa bayi di buaian, membuat mereka tak bisa tidur dan menangis. Kata maruda juga berarti orang yang membosankan, suka menggerutu, atau lamban.",
      "en": "Maruda is a female demon of Slavic mythology who torments babies in their cradles, keeping them from sleep and making them cry. The word maruda also means a dull, grumbling or sluggish person.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "maruda-slavic-demon-c01",
        "maruda-slavic-demon-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada abad ke-19 orang memakai cara magis untuk melindungi bayi: mangkuk berisi air dengan benda-benda seperti kumparan, sendok, benang, atau jahitan diletakkan di kamar agar demon sibuk dan membiarkan bayi tidur. Orang juga menempatkan sembilan boneka kain di sekeliling buaian, dan di beberapa daerah Lublin anak perempuan dilarang bermain boneka karena maruda dipercaya bersemayam di dalamnya.",
      "en": "In the 19th century people used magical means to protect babies: a bowl of water with objects such as spindles, spoons, thread or sewing was set in the room to keep the demon busy so the baby could sleep. People also placed nine rag dolls around the cradle, and in parts of the Lublin region girls were forbidden to play with dolls because a maruda was thought to lodge in them.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "maruda-slavic-demon-c01",
      "source_id": "maruda-slavic-demon-s1",
      "quote": "Maruda is a demon in Slavic mythology in female form, who is said to have harassed children in a cradle.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maruda adalah demon berwujud perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia yang konon mengganggu bayi di buaian.",
        "en": "Maruda is a female demon of Slavic mythology said to harass children in the cradle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruda-slavic-demon-c02",
      "source_id": "maruda-slavic-demon-s1",
      "quote": "In the 19th century it was believed that using magical methods was necessary to deal with the demon successfully. For instance, people put three spindles, three spoons and sewing into the bowl of water as to make the harasser busy enough to let the baby sleep undisturbed.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada abad ke-19 diyakini bahwa cara-cara magis diperlukan untuk menghadapi demon ini, misalnya menaruh tiga kumparan, tiga sendok, dan jahitan dalam mangkuk berisi air agar pengganggu itu sibuk dan bayi bisa tidur tenang.",
        "en": "In the 19th century magical methods were thought necessary to deal with the demon, for example putting three spindles, three spoons and sewing into a bowl of water to keep the harasser busy so the baby could sleep undisturbed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruda-slavic-demon-c03",
      "source_id": "maruda-slavic-demon-s2",
      "quote": "Maruda (człowiek nudny, gderający, ślamazarny, opieszały, uciążliwy, żmudny, guzdrała) – demoniczna postać kobieca dręcząca dzieci w kołyskach, niepozwalająca im zasnąć, doprowadzająca do płaczu.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maruda (kata yang juga berarti orang yang membosankan, suka menggerutu, lamban, dan merepotkan) adalah sosok demonik perempuan yang menyiksa bayi di buaian, membuat mereka tak bisa tidur dan menangis.",
        "en": "Maruda (also meaning a dull, grumbling, sluggish, tiresome person) is a demonic female figure who torments children in cradles, keeping them from sleep and making them cry."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruda-slavic-demon-c04",
      "source_id": "maruda-slavic-demon-s2",
      "quote": "kładziono w pokoju, gdzie spało niemowlę, miskę z wodą, a w niej kłębek nici, łyżkę i wrzeciono; zajęty tymi akcesoriami demon zostawiał dziecko w spokoju",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di kamar tempat bayi tidur diletakkan mangkuk berisi air dengan gulungan benang, sendok, dan kumparan; demon yang sibuk dengan benda-benda itu akan membiarkan bayi tenang.",
        "en": "In the room where the baby slept, a bowl of water holding a ball of thread, a spoon and a spindle was placed; busy with these things, the demon left the child in peace."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruda-slavic-demon-c05",
      "source_id": "maruda-slavic-demon-s2",
      "quote": "robiono z gałganków dziewięć lalek i obsadzano nimi kolebkę dziecka, zaczynając od główek.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang juga membuat sembilan boneka dari kain perca dan menempatkannya di sekeliling buaian bayi, dimulai dari bagian kepala.",
        "en": "People also made nine rag dolls and placed them around the baby's cradle, starting at the head end."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruda-slavic-demon-c06",
      "source_id": "maruda-slavic-demon-s2",
      "quote": "w niektórych okolicach w Lubelskiem zabraniano dziewczynkom bawić się lalkami, sądząc, że w nich siedzi maruda, która dusi dzieci.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di beberapa daerah di wilayah Lublin, anak perempuan dilarang bermain boneka karena dipercaya maruda bersemayam di dalamnya dan mencekik anak-anak.",
        "en": "In some parts of the Lublin region girls were forbidden to play with dolls, since a maruda that strangles children was thought to sit in them."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Menninkäinen adalah roh dalam mitologi Finlandia yang awalnya tinggal di tempat terpencil dan ramah kepada manusia, kemudian digambarkan sebagai makhluk mirip goblin penjaga harta.",
    "en": "The menninkäinen are spirits of Finnish mythology, originally dwelling in isolated places and friendly to humans, later pictured as goblin-like treasure guardians.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "menninkainen-c01",
      "menninkainen-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi dan cerita Finlandia, menninkäinen awalnya digambarkan sebagai roh yang tinggal di tempat terpencil dan umumnya ramah kepada manusia. Seiring waktu mereka digambarkan sebagai makhluk mirip goblin yang, seperti leprechaun, menjaga harta dan menyukai teka-teki serta permainan adu kuasa. Sumber-sumber berbeda pendapat tentang tempat tinggal mereka: sebagian menyebut mereka \"pekerja industri\" di kota, yang lain penghuni hutan.",
      "en": "In Finnish mythology and lore the menninkäinen were originally described as spirits living in isolated places and generally friendly to humans. Over time they came to be described as goblin-like creatures that, like leprechauns, guard treasures and enjoy riddles and games of dominance. Sources disagree on where they live: some call them \"industrial workers\" in towns, others forest dwellers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "menninkainen-c01",
        "menninkainen-c02",
        "menninkainen-c03"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Muroni adalah vampir dalam mitologi Wallachia yang bisa berubah menjadi berbagai hewan; namanya ubahan Inggris lama dari moroi Rumania.",
    "en": "The Muroni is a vampire of Wallachian mythology able to turn into various animals; its name is an old English corruption of the Romanian moroi.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "muroni-c01",
      "muroni-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Muroni atau Murony adalah vampir dalam mitologi Wallachia. Ia mampu berubah menjadi berbagai hewan, sehingga serangannya sering dikira serangan hewan, dan satu-satunya tanda kehadirannya adalah kehilangan darah yang luar biasa. Muroni dipercaya sebenarnya hantu pengisap darah yang mengambil wujud makhluk lain agar mudah memangsa.",
      "en": "The Muroni or Murony is a vampire of Wallachian mythology. It could turn into various animals, so its attacks were often taken for animal attacks, the only sign of its presence being exceptional blood loss. It was believed to be really a blood-sucking ghost that took other forms to feed more easily.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "muroni-c01",
        "muroni-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama murony dan muroni adalah bentuk ubahan Inggris lama dari istilah Rumania moroi, yang terutama dipakai dalam dialek selatan seperti di Wallachia. Moroi berbeda dari strigoi karena fana, tetapi bila dibunuh strigoi ia menjadi strigoi yang abadi. Ia bisa berubah menjadi anjing, kucing, kodok, atau serangga, dan hanya bisa dimusnahkan dengan menggali jasadnya, menusuk dahi dengan jarum, menancapkan pasak kayu ke jantung, lalu membakarnya.",
      "en": "The names murony and muroni are old English corruptions of the Romanian term moroi, used mostly in southern dialects such as in Wallachia. The moroi differs from the strigoi in being mortal, but if killed by a strigoi it becomes an immortal strigoi. It can turn into a dog, cat, toad or insects, and could be destroyed only by digging up the body, driving a needle into the forehead, staking the heart and burning it.",
      "claim_ids": [

      "source_id": "muroni-s1",
      "quote": "The Muroni (or Murony) is a vampire in Wallachian mythology. It has the ability to transform into a variety of different animals. Due to this characteristic, a Muroni attack could be very hard to identify and could often be thought of as an animal attack. The only sign that a Muroni was there at all was an exceptional amount of blood loss.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Muroni (atau Murony) adalah vampir dalam mitologi Wallachia yang mampu berubah menjadi berbagai hewan, sehingga serangannya sulit dikenali dan sering dikira serangan hewan; satu-satunya tanda kehadirannya adalah kehilangan darah yang luar biasa banyak.",
        "en": "The Muroni (or Murony) is a vampire of Wallachian mythology able to transform into various animals, so its attacks were hard to identify and often taken for animal attacks; the only sign of its presence was an exceptional loss of blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "muroni-c02",
      "source_id": "muroni-s1",
      "quote": "The Muroni is one of many vampire legends which was believed to be a blood-sucking ghost in reality, only taking on the form of other beings to make feeding easier.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Muroni termasuk legenda vampir yang dipercaya sebenarnya hantu pengisap darah, yang hanya mengambil wujud makhluk lain agar lebih mudah memangsa.",
        "en": "The Muroni is one of many vampire legends believed to be really a blood-sucking ghost, taking other forms only to make feeding easier."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "muroni-c03",
      "source_id": "muroni-s2",
      "quote": "Murony ou muroni sont des déformations anglaises anciennes du terme roumain moroï, proche mais différent des strigoï : il est mortel alors que le strigoï est immortel, mais si un moroï est tué par un strigoï, il devient strigoï et immortel à son tour.",
      "locator": "introduction",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Murony atau muroni adalah bentuk ubahan Inggris lama dari istilah Rumania moroi, yang dekat tetapi berbeda dari strigoi: moroi fana sedangkan strigoi abadi, tetapi bila moroi dibunuh strigoi ia menjadi strigoi yang abadi pula.",
        "en": "Murony or muroni are old English corruptions of the Romanian term moroi, close to but distinct from the strigoi: it is mortal while the strigoi is immortal, but if a moroi is killed by a strigoi it becomes an immortal strigoi in turn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "muroni-c04",
      "source_id": "muroni-s2",
      "quote": "Elles ne pouvaient être détruites que si leur corps était déterré, qu’on leur enfonce une aiguille dans le front et qu’on transperce leur cœur avec un pieu en bois avant de les brûler.",
      "locator": "introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk-makhluk ini hanya bisa dimusnahkan bila jasadnya digali, dahinya ditusuk jarum, dan jantungnya ditembus pasak kayu sebelum dibakar.",
        "en": "These creatures could be destroyed only if their body was dug up, a needle driven into the forehead and the heart pierced with a wooden stake before burning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "muroni-c05",
      "source_id": "muroni-s2",
      "quote": "Le moroi peut se métamorphoser en chien, en chat, en crapaud, en grenouille, en puce, en pou, en punaise ou en araignée.",
      "locator": "introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Moroi bisa berubah menjadi anjing, kucing, kodok, katak, kutu loncat, kutu rambut, kepinding, atau laba-laba.",
        "en": "The moroi can turn into a dog, cat, toad, frog, flea, louse, bedbug or spider."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "muroni-c06",
      "source_id": "muroni-s2",
      "quote": "Moroï est plutôt utilisé dans les parlers roumains méridionaux, notamment en Valachie",
      "locator": "introduction",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata moroi lebih banyak dipakai dalam dialek Rumania selatan, terutama di Wallachia.",
        "en": "The word moroi is used mostly in southern Romanian dialects, notably in Wallachia."
      }
    }

      "en": "Obyda is a female figure of Udmurt mythology. In folklore she appears in two guises: as a forest spirit who helps people and as a witch-hag, the Udmurt counterpart of Baba Yaga. Folklore texts show that she lives in the forest in a large family.",

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Otso adalah roh kolektif seluruh beruang dalam mitologi Finlandia, raja suci para hewan dan pemimpin hutan yang namanya tidak boleh disebut langsung.",
    "en": "Otso is the collective spirit of all bears in Finnish mythology, sacred king of animals and leader of the forest, whose true name must never be spoken.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "otso-c01",
      "otso-c02",
      "otso-c07"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Finlandia, Otso adalah beruang, raja suci para hewan dan pemimpin hutan, yang sangat ditakuti dan dihormati suku-suku Finlandia kuno. Ia bukan roh seekor beruang tertentu, melainkan roh kolektif seluruh beruang. Nama-nama seperti Otso (\"si dahi lebar\"), Kontio, Metsän kuningas (\"raja hutan\"), dan Mesikämmen (\"cakar madu\") adalah sebutan kiasan, karena roh beruang tidak boleh disebut namanya secara langsung.",
      "en": "In Finnish mythology Otso is a bear revered as the holy king of the animals who leads the forest, greatly feared and honoured by the old Finnish tribes. He is not a particular bear's spirit but the collective spirit of all bears. Names such as Otso (\"broad forehead\"), Kontio, Metsän kuningas (\"king of the forest\") and Mesikämmen (\"honey paw\") are circumlocutions, because the bear spirit must never be named directly.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "otso-c01",
        "otso-c02",
        "otso-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal-usul Otso dikisahkan berbeda-beda: sebagian kisah menyebut ia lahir dari wol yang dilempar dewa cuaca Ukko ke laut, kisah lain dari rasi Biduk. Mitos tertua menceritakan pernikahannya dengan ibu purba seluruh manusia, yang tercermin dalam ritual Peijaiset: bila beruang dibunuh, tengkoraknya dipasang di pohon pinus dan rohnya secara simbolis dikembalikan kepada keluarganya.",
      "en": "Otso's origin is told in different ways: some stories say he was born from wool that the weather god Ukko threw into the sea, others from the Ursa Major constellation. The oldest myths tell of his marriage to the primordial mother of all people, reflected in the Peijaiset ritual: when a bear was killed its skull was mounted on a pine and its spirit symbolically returned to its family.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "otso-c03",
        "otso-c04",
        "otso-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nyanyian suci pemujaan beruang dikenal pada banyak bangsa Fino-Ugria, dari Finlandia hingga Khanty dan Mansi. Otso muncul dalam epos Kalevala, beruang menjadi hewan nasional Finlandia, dan Otso juga dipuja dalam neopaganisme Finlandia modern.",
      "en": "Sacred bear-cult songs are known among many Finno-Ugric peoples, from the Finns to the Khanty and Mansi. Otso appears in the Kalevala epic, the bear is Finland's national animal, and Otso is also worshipped in modern Finnish neopaganism.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "otso-c01",
        "otso-c06",
        "otso-c08",
        "otso-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Beruang adalah hewan paling dihormati dalam kepercayaan Finlandia kuno; ritual Peijaiset mengembalikan roh beruang yang dibunuh kepada keluarganya.",
    "en": "The bear was the most revered animal in old Finnish belief; the Peijaiset ritual returned the spirit of a killed bear to its family.",
