```json
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "otso-c01",
      "source_id": "otso-s1",
      "quote": "In Finnish mythology, Otso (also known as Karhu, Ohto, Kontio, Metsän kuningas, and Mesikämmen) is a bear, the sacred king of animals and leader of the forest. It was deeply feared and respected by old Finnish tribes. Otso appears in the Finnish national epic, the Kalevala.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Finlandia, Otso (juga Karhu, Ohto, Kontio, Metsän kuningas, dan Mesikämmen) adalah beruang, raja suci para hewan dan pemimpin hutan, yang sangat ditakuti dan dihormati suku-suku Finlandia kuno, dan ia muncul dalam epos nasional Kalevala.",
        "en": "In Finnish mythology Otso (also Karhu, Ohto, Kontio, Metsän kuningas and Mesikämmen) is a bear, the sacred king of animals and leader of the forest, deeply feared and respected by old Finnish tribes, and he appears in the national epic Kalevala."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otso-c02",
      "source_id": "otso-s1",
      "quote": "Otso is not a particular individual bear spirit, but rather the collective animistic spirit of all bears.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Otso bukan roh seekor beruang tertentu, melainkan roh animistis kolektif seluruh beruang.",
        "en": "Otso is not a particular bear's spirit but the collective animistic spirit of all bears."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otso-c03",
      "source_id": "otso-s1",
      "quote": "Some stories tell of how Ukko, the god of weather, threw wool into the sea and how Otso was born from the bits of wool that reached the shore. Other stories tell of how Otso was born from the Ursa Major constellation.",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kisah menyebut Ukko, dewa cuaca, melemparkan wol ke laut dan Otso lahir dari serpihan wol yang terdampar di pantai; kisah lain menyebut Otso lahir dari rasi bintang Biduk (Ursa Major).",
        "en": "Some stories tell that Ukko, the weather god, threw wool into the sea and Otso was born from the bits of wool that reached the shore; others say Otso was born from the Ursa Major constellation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otso-c04",
      "source_id": "otso-s1",
      "quote": "The most ancient myths surrounding Otso tell of the marriage between Otso and the primordial mother of all men. This relationship was reflected in the ritual of Peijaiset, in which the bear spirit was symbolically returned to its family through marriage.",
      "locator": "Myths",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitos tertua tentang Otso mengisahkan pernikahan Otso dengan ibu purba seluruh manusia, yang tercermin dalam ritual Peijaiset, ketika roh beruang secara simbolis dikembalikan kepada keluarganya melalui pernikahan.",
        "en": "The oldest myths about Otso tell of his marriage to the primordial mother of all people, reflected in the Peijaiset ritual, in which the bear spirit was symbolically returned to its family through marriage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otso-c05",
      "source_id": "otso-s1",
      "quote": "If a bear had to be killed by ancient Finns, a sacred ritual of Peijaiset was held. In it, the bear's skull was mounted on a pine tree.",
      "locator": "Peijaiset",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila orang Finlandia kuno terpaksa membunuh beruang, diadakan ritual suci Peijaiset, di mana tengkorak beruang dipasang di pohon pinus.",
        "en": "If the ancient Finns had to kill a bear, a sacred Peijaiset ritual was held, in which the bear's skull was mounted on a pine tree."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otso-c06",
      "source_id": "otso-s1",
      "quote": "Besides being worshipped by historical Finnish pagans, Otso is also worshipped in modern Finnish neopaganism, and the neopagan organization Karhun kansa is named after the bear.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selain dipuja kaum pagan Finlandia di masa lalu, Otso juga dipuja dalam neopaganisme Finlandia modern, dan organisasi neopagan Karhun kansa dinamai menurut beruang.",
        "en": "Besides being worshipped by historical Finnish pagans, Otso is also worshipped in modern Finnish neopaganism, and the neopagan organisation Karhun kansa is named after the bear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otso-c07",
      "source_id": "otso-s2",
      "quote": "Otso (« le front large »), Ohto, Kontio, metsän kuningas (« le roi de la forêt »), et mesikämmen (« pattes de miel »), sont les principales circonlocutions utilisées dans la mythologie finnoise, pour désigner l'esprit de l'ours, que l'on ne doit jamais nommer.",
      "locator": "introduction",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Otso (\"si dahi lebar\"), Ohto, Kontio, metsän kuningas (\"raja hutan\"), dan mesikämmen (\"cakar madu\") adalah sebutan kiasan utama dalam mitologi Finlandia untuk roh beruang, yang tidak boleh disebut namanya secara langsung.",
        "en": "Otso (\"broad forehead\"), Ohto, Kontio, metsän kuningas (\"king of the forest\") and mesikämmen (\"honey paw\") are the main circumlocutions in Finnish mythology for the spirit of the bear, which must never be named."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otso-c08",
      "source_id": "otso-s1",
      "quote": "Due to the importance of the bear spirit in historical Finnish paganism, bears are still considered by many Finns to be kings of the forest, and the bear is even the national animal of Finland.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena pentingnya roh beruang dalam paganisme Finlandia masa lalu, banyak orang Finlandia masih menganggap beruang raja hutan, dan beruang bahkan menjadi hewan nasional Finlandia.",
        "en": "Because of the bear spirit's importance in historical Finnish paganism, many Finns still consider bears kings of the forest, and the bear is even Finland's national animal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otso-c09",
      "source_id": "otso-s2",
      "quote": "Les chants sacrés du culte de l'ours concernent la plupart des peuples de tradition finno-ougrienne : finnois, estoniens, samis, mordves, khantys, mansis.",
      "locator": "introduction",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyanyian suci pemujaan beruang dikenal pada sebagian besar bangsa bertradisi Fino-Ugria: Finlandia, Estonia, Sami, Mordvin, Khanty, dan Mansi.",
        "en": "The sacred songs of the bear cult concern most peoples of Finno-Ugric tradition: Finns, Estonians, Sami, Mordvins, Khanty and Mansi."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
      "id": "Pustecki atau Pustecký adalah roh tambang dalam kepercayaan rakyat Polandia dan Ceko yang menghuni tambang batu bara di sekitar Karviná di Silesia Cieszyn. Ia tampak seperti penambang berseragam dengan janggut putih panjang, lampu karbit emas di helm, dan beliung perak di tangan. Wataknya mirip skarbnik.",
      "en": "Pustecki or Pustecký is a mine spirit of Polish and Czech folk belief living in the coal mines around Karviná in Cieszyn Silesia.       "title": "Пустецкий",

      "title": "Pustecki",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (pl)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "pl",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "pustecki-c01",
      "source_id": "pustecki-s1",
      "quote": "— дух или призрак в польской и чешской мифологиях, обитающий на территории угольной шахты рядом с Карвиной в Тешинской Силезии. Выглядит подобно шахтёру, в форме, с длинной седой бородой. На каску у него надета золотая карбидная лампа, в руках он держит серебряную кирку. По характеру Пустецкого связывают со скарбником.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pustecki adalah roh atau hantu dalam mitologi Polandia dan Ceko yang menghuni tambang batu bara dekat Karviná di Silesia Cieszyn; ia tampak seperti penambang berseragam berjanggut putih panjang, dengan lampu karbit emas di helm dan beliung perak di tangan, dan wataknya dikaitkan dengan skarbnik.",
        "en": "Pustecki is a spirit or ghost of Polish and Czech mythology living in a coal mine near Karviná in Cieszyn Silesia; he looks like a miner in uniform with a long grey beard, a golden carbide lamp on his helmet and a silver pick in his hands, and in character he is linked with the skarbnik."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pustecki-c02",
      "source_id": "pustecki-s1",
      "quote": "Добрый и заботливый дух. Всегда помогает трудолюбивым шахтёрам, награждает их золотом. Выручает потерявшихся под землёй, выводит их на поверхность. Заботится о душах умерших в шахтах.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia roh yang baik dan penuh perhatian: selalu menolong penambang yang rajin dan menghadiahi mereka emas, menyelamatkan yang tersesat di bawah tanah dan menuntun mereka ke permukaan, serta merawat jiwa mereka yang tewas di tambang.",
        "en": "He is a kind and caring spirit: he always helps hard-working miners and rewards them with gold, rescues those lost underground and leads them to the surface, and cares for the souls of those who died in the mines."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pustecki-c03",
      "source_id": "pustecki-s1",
      "quote": "Пустецкий предупреждает шахтёров о возможной опасности во время работы. Он превращается в какое-нибудь маленькое животное, например, в мышь, и сильно визжит.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pustecki memperingatkan penambang akan bahaya saat bekerja dengan berubah menjadi hewan kecil, misalnya tikus, lalu mencicit keras.",
        "en": "Pustecki warns miners of possible danger at work by turning into a small animal, such as a mouse, and squeaking loudly."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pustecki-c04",
      "source_id": "pustecki-s2",
      "quote": "Pustecki, cz. Pustecký - důlní duch – duch z wierzeń ludowych Śląska Cieszyńskiego, zamieszkujący kopalnie węgla kamiennego w okolicach Karwiny. W swym charakterze podobny skarbnikowi.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pustecki (bahasa Ceko: Pustecký) adalah roh tambang dalam kepercayaan rakyat Silesia Cieszyn yang menghuni tambang batu bara di sekitar Karviná, dengan watak mirip skarbnik.",
        "en": "Pustecki (Czech Pustecký) is a mine spirit of the folk beliefs of Cieszyn Silesia, inhabiting the coal mines around Karviná, similar in character to the skarbnik."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pustecki-c05",
      "source_id": "pustecki-s2",
      "quote": "Nie tolerował jednak pewnych zachowań, m.in. gwizdania w kopalniach, za co karał.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namun ia tidak menoleransi perilaku tertentu, antara lain bersiul di tambang, dan menghukum pelakunya.",
        "en": "However, he did not tolerate certain behaviour, including whistling in the mines, and punished it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pustecki-c06",
      "source_id": "pustecki-s2",
      "quote": "Pustecki pojawiał się również na powierzchni, niejednokrotnie ostrzegając zdążających do pracy górników przed mającymi nastąpić katastrofami.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pustecki juga muncul di permukaan, berkali-kali memperingatkan penambang yang hendak berangkat kerja akan bencana yang akan terjadi.",
        "en": "Pustecki also appeared above ground, more than once warning miners on their way to work of disasters about to happen."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "countries",
      "searched": "Kawasan Karviná kini di Republik Ceko, tetapi negara modern tidak dinyatakan langsung di kutipan."
    },
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia ru dan pl."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}
```

```json
    {
      "id": "sexual-vampire-c01",
      "source_id": "sexual-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "A sexual vampire practiced esoteric Daoist sexual techniques where one partner purportedly strengthened their own body by absorbing the other partner's qi (\"life force\") and jing (\"sexual energy\") without emitting any of one's own.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vampir seksual menjalankan teknik seksual Tao yang esoterik, di mana satu pihak konon menguatkan tubuhnya dengan menyerap qi (\"daya hidup\") dan jing (\"energi seksual\") pasangannya tanpa melepaskan miliknya sendiri.",
        "en": "A sexual vampire practised esoteric Daoist sexual techniques in which one partner supposedly strengthened their own body by absorbing the other's qi (\"life force\") and jing (\"sexual energy\") without emitting any of their own."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sexual-vampire-c02",
      "source_id": "sexual-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "Practitioners believed that sexual vampirism could enable them to maintain eternal youth and become a Daoist xian (\"transcendent; immortal\"). However, an unwitting victim repeatedly subjected to sexual vampirism would supposedly weaken and die.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para pelakunya percaya vampirisme seksual dapat membuat mereka awet muda dan menjadi xian (\"makhluk transenden; abadi\") Tao, sedangkan korban yang tak sadar dan berulang kali menjadi sasarannya konon akan melemah dan mati.",
        "en": "Practitioners believed sexual vampirism could keep them eternally young and make them a Daoist xian (\"transcendent; immortal\"), while an unwitting victim repeatedly subjected to it would supposedly weaken and die."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sexual-vampire-c03",
      "source_id": "sexual-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "Myths about Chinese gods and immortals recount sexual vampires who allegedly became xian transcendents. For instance, after having sexual intercourse with 1,200 young women, the legendary Yellow Emperor achieved spiritual transcendence and ascended into heaven.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitos tentang dewa dan makhluk abadi Tiongkok menceritakan vampir seksual yang konon menjadi xian; misalnya Kaisar Kuning yang legendaris disebut mencapai transendensi dan naik ke surga setelah berhubungan dengan 1.200 perempuan muda.",
        "en": "Myths about Chinese gods and immortals tell of sexual vampires who allegedly became xian; for instance, the legendary Yellow Emperor reached transcendence and ascended to heaven after intercourse with 1,200 young women."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sexual-vampire-c04",
      "source_id": "sexual-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "Two Chinese mythological creatures are comparable to sex vampires. A jiangshi ... kills people to absorb their qi",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dua makhluk mitologi Tiongkok sebanding dengan vampir seksual; salah satunya jiangshi, yang membunuh orang untuk menyerap qi mereka.",
        "en": "Two Chinese mythological creatures are comparable to sex vampires; one is the jiangshi, which kills people to absorb their qi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sexual-vampire-c05",
      "source_id": "sexual-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "A Chinese sexual vampire is analogous to English terms like psychic vampire, energy vampire, succubus, or incubus. These mythical beings feed on human vital forces, similar to traditional vampires (sanguinarians or hematophages) who purportedly feed on blood.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vampir seksual Tiongkok sepadan dengan istilah Inggris seperti psychic vampire, energy vampire, succubus, atau incubus, yaitu makhluk mitos yang memangsa daya hidup manusia, mirip vampir tradisional yang konon mengisap darah.",
        "en": "A Chinese sexual vampire is analogous to English terms such as psychic vampire, energy vampire, succubus or incubus, mythical beings that feed on human vital force, like traditional vampires that supposedly feed on blood."
      }
    }
```

```json
      "en": "Why might a folk-song figure be read as both a person and a bird?"
    }
  ],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "siuru-s1",
      "url": "https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A1%D0%B8%D1%83%D1%80%D1%83_(%D1%8D%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B8%D1%84%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B3%D0%B8%D1%8F)",
      "title": "Сиуру (эстонская мифология)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ru)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ru",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "siuru-c01",
      "source_id": "siuru-s1",
      "quote": "— сказочная птица в старинных эстонских народных песнях. Упоминается также в девятнадцатой песни эстонского народного эпоса «Калевипоэг».",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Siuru (Siurulind) adalah burung dongeng dalam lagu-lagu rakyat Estonia lama, dan juga disebut dalam nyanyian kesembilan belas epos rakyat Estonia Kalevipoeg.",
        "en": "Siuru (Siurulind) is a fabulous bird of old Estonian folk songs, also mentioned in the nineteenth canto of the Estonian folk epic Kalevipoeg."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "siuru-c02",
      "source_id": "siuru-s1",
      "quote": "связывают с финским словом kiuru, которое означает «жаворонок».",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Siuru dikaitkan dengan kata Finlandia kiuru, yang berarti \"burung lark\".",
        "en": "The name Siuru is linked with the Finnish word kiuru, meaning \"lark\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "siuru-c03",
      "source_id": "siuru-s1",
      "quote": "Человек Сиуру или птица — этот вопрос много обсуждался эстонцами. Те, кто считает, что Сиуру — человек, ссылаются на то, что Крейцвальд называет её «дочкой Таары».",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apakah Siuru manusia atau burung banyak diperdebatkan orang Estonia; mereka yang menganggapnya manusia merujuk pada Kreutzwald yang menyebutnya \"putri Taara\".",
        "en": "Whether Siuru is a person or a bird has been much debated by Estonians; those who think Siuru is a person point out that Kreutzwald calls her \"the daughter of Taara\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "siuru-c04",
      "source_id": "siuru-s1",
      "quote": "Птица Сиуру, дочка Таары, Синекрылая летунья, С шёлковыми пёрышками, Проклевалась без родимой, Выросла без милых братев И без ласковых сестричек ... Это видел старый Уку, Подарил тебе он крылья, Сделал крылья легче ветра, Чтоб на них дитя скользило, Чтоб на крылышках летало",
      "locator": "«Калевипоэг». Песнь девятнадцатая",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam nyanyian Kalevipoeg: \"Burung Siuru, putri Taara, si penerbang bersayap biru berbulu sutra, menetas tanpa ibu, tumbuh tanpa saudara dan saudari\"; Uku tua melihatnya dan memberinya sayap yang lebih ringan dari angin agar sang anak melayang dan terbang.",
        "en": "In the Kalevipoeg canto: \"The bird Siuru, daughter of Taara, blue-winged flier with silken feathers, hatched without a mother, grew up without brothers or kind sisters\"; old Uku saw this and gave her wings lighter than the wind so the child could glide and fly."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Stolem adalah raksasa dalam legenda Kashubia yang dahulu menghuni tanah Kashubia dan dipercaya membentuk bukit, tanjung, serta batu-batu besar di sana.",
    "en": "The stolems are giants of Kashubian legend who once lived in Kashubia and are believed to have shaped its hills, spits and great boulders.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "stolems-c01",
      "stolems-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam legenda Kashubia, stolem adalah raksasa yang berabad-abad lalu menghuni tanah Kashubia. Mereka seperti manusia tetapi begitu besar hingga kepala mereka melampaui pohon pinus tertinggi, dan kekuatan mereka konon berasal dari daging dan susu kuda. Mereka punya keluarga biasa, gemar memamerkan kekuatan, dan banyak batu besar dianggap sisa lemparan mereka saat bertarung.",
      "en": "In Kashubian legend the stolems are giants who centuries ago lived in Kashubia. They were like people but so huge that their heads rose above the tallest pines, and their strength was said to come from horse meat and horse milk. They had ordinary families, loved to show off their strength, and many great boulders are held to be stones they threw in their fights.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "stolems-c01",
        "stolems-c02",
        "stolems-c06",
        "stolems-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Stolem dianggap membentuk bentang alam Kashubia, menciptakan bukit, tanjung di danau, dan beting di laut. Menurut satu legenda yang dikutip Józef Ceynowa, mereka dihukum para dewa karena kejahatan dan diubah menjadi lingkaran batu, sementara dalam kisah lain Ceynowa mereka justru makhluk baik yang menolong manusia. Lama-kelamaan mereka punah.",
      "en": "The stolems are credited with shaping the Kashubian landscape, creating hills, lake spits and sandbanks. In one legend quoted by Józef Ceynowa they were punished by the gods for their wickedness and turned into stone circles, while in another of Ceynowa's tales they are good beings who helped people. In time they died out.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "stolems-c04",
        "stolems-c08"
      ]

      "id": "Penulis Kashubia seperti Aleksander Majkowski menjadikan stolem simbol kejayaan lama Pomerania Kashubia.",
      "en": "Kashubian writers such as Aleksander Majkowski made the stolem a symbol of the former might of Kashubian Pomerania.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "stolems-c01",
      "source_id": "stolems-s1",
      "quote": "Stolemy (kaszub. Stolemë) – w kaszubskich legendach olbrzymy, które przed wiekami zamieszkiwały tereny Kaszub. Pozostałością po ich obecności są jakoby liczne wielkie głazy, które stolemowie porozrzucali podczas walk lub zawodów siłowych.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Stolem (bahasa Kashubia: Stolemë) dalam legenda Kashubia adalah raksasa yang berabad-abad lalu menghuni tanah Kashubia; banyak batu besar konon merupakan sisa kehadiran mereka, dilemparkan saat bertarung atau beradu kekuatan.",
        "en": "The stolems (Kashubian Stolemë) are, in Kashubian legend, giants who centuries ago lived in Kashubia; many large boulders are said to be traces of them, thrown about during fights or contests of strength."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stolems-c02",
      "source_id": "stolems-s1",
      "quote": "Stolemowie odznaczali się rzekomo nadzwyczajnym wzrostem i siłą, byli także okrutni i prześladowali ludzi. Lubili się popisywać swoją siłą wobec siebie i ludzi. Miewali normalne rodziny, były więc i stolemki, i stolemiątka.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Stolem konon luar biasa tinggi dan kuat, kejam, dan suka menindas manusia; mereka gemar memamerkan kekuatan di antara sesamanya dan kepada manusia, serta punya keluarga biasa, termasuk stolem perempuan dan anak-anak stolem.",
        "en": "The stolems were said to be extraordinarily tall and strong, cruel and oppressive to people; they liked to show off their strength to one another and to humans, and had ordinary families, with female stolems and stolem children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stolems-c03",
      "source_id": "stolems-s1",
      "quote": "Przypisuje się im uformowanie obecnego krajobrazu Kaszub. Tworzyli wzgórza, mierzeje na jeziorach czy mielizny na morzu. Według jednego z podań przytoczonych przez Józefa Ceynowę za swoją niegodziwość zostali ukarani przez bogów i zamienieni w kamienne kręgi.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dianggap membentuk bentang alam Kashubia sekarang, menciptakan bukit, tanjung pasir di danau, dan beting di laut; menurut satu legenda yang dikutip Józef Ceynowa, mereka dihukum para dewa karena kejahatannya dan diubah menjadi lingkaran batu.",
        "en": "They are credited with shaping today's Kashubian landscape, creating hills, spits on lakes and sandbanks at sea; according to a legend quoted by Józef Ceynowa, they were punished by the gods for their wickedness and turned into stone circles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stolems-c04",
      "source_id": "stolems-s1",
      "quote": "Z kolei w zbiorze legend kaszubskich Dobro zwycięża Ceynowa przedstawił inne opowiadanie, wedle którego stolemowie mieli być dobrymi istotami, pomagającymi ludziom.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebaliknya, dalam kumpulan legenda Kashubia Dobro zwycięża, Ceynowa menyajikan kisah lain yang menyebut stolem sebagai makhluk baik yang menolong manusia.",
        "en": "Conversely, in the Kashubian legend collection Dobro zwycięża, Ceynowa presented another tale in which the stolems were good beings who helped people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stolems-c05",
      "source_id": "stolems-s1",
      "quote": "Inni pisarze kaszubscy, z Aleksandrem Majkowskim na czele, wielokrotnie wykorzystywali motyw stolema, widząc w nim chętnie symbol dawnej potęgi kaszubskiego Pomorza.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penulis Kashubia lain, terutama Aleksander Majkowski, berulang kali memakai motif stolem sebagai simbol kejayaan lama Pomerania Kashubia.",
        "en": "Other Kashubian writers, led by Aleksander Majkowski, repeatedly used the stolem motif as a symbol of the former might of Kashubian Pomerania."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stolems-c06",
      "source_id": "stolems-s2",
      "quote": "Stolem – pòdług Kaszëbów òn bëł jak lëdze, ale tak wiôldżi, że jegò głowa przewiższała nôwiąkszé chójczi.",
      "locator": "wstãp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut orang Kashubia, stolem seperti manusia tetapi begitu besar sehingga kepalanya melampaui pohon pinus tertinggi.",
        "en": "According to the Kashubians, a stolem was like a person but so huge that his head rose above the tallest pines."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stolems-c07",
      "source_id": "stolems-s2",
      "quote": "Stolemi mielë taką mòc, bò jedlë kóńsczé miãso i pilë kóńsczé mlékò.",
      "locator": "wstãp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Stolem sekuat itu karena mereka makan daging kuda dan minum susu kuda.",
        "en": "The stolems were so strong because they ate horse meat and drank horse milk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stolems-c08",
      "source_id": "stolems-s2",
      "quote": "Òni ùsëpale òstrowë i retczi na kaszëbsczëch jezorach, a nawetka całi je zasëpale i tam dzys są łączi. Z czasem stolemi wëmarlë.",
      "locator": "wstãp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka menimbun pulau-pulau dan beting di danau-danau Kashubia, bahkan menimbun seluruh danau hingga kini menjadi padang rumput; lama-kelamaan para stolem punah.",
        "en": "They heaped up islands and shoals in the Kashubian lakes, even filling whole lakes that are meadows today; in time the stolems died out."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Stricha atau Dolya adalah dewi nasib baik proto-Ukraina, perwujudan Rozhanytsa, yang menentukan nasib manusia saat lahir dan dibayangkan memintal benang emas takdir.",
    "en": "Stricha or Dolya is the proto-Ukrainian goddess of good fate, an incarnation of Rozhanytsa, who sets a person's destiny at birth and is pictured spinning the golden thread of fate.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "stricha-c01",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Stricha atau Dolya adalah dewi nasib baik proto-Ukraina, salah satu perwujudan Rozhanytsa. Ia meramalkan masa depan pasangan suami istri dan kelahiran anak, menentukan tujuan hidup seseorang, dan saat kelahiran menganugerahi bayi dengan segala kebajikan. Ia dibayangkan sebagai perempuan muda jelita yang memintal benang emas nasib manusia, dengan lambang kumparan dan gulungan benang.",
      "en": "Stricha or Dolya is a goddess of good fortune in proto-Ukrainian belief, regarded as one form of Rozhanytsa. She foretells the future of married couples and the birth of children, determines a person's purpose and at birth endows babies with every virtue. She is pictured as a beautiful young woman spinning the golden thread of human fate, her symbols being the spindle and the ball of thread.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "stricha-c01",
        "stricha-c02",
        "stricha-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dolya harus ditenangkan dengan hadiah seperti bubur, wol, benang, roti, madu, dan susu; secara harfiah dolya berarti \"bagian\". Menurut kalender pagan yang direkonstruksi, hari Stritennia pada 1–2 Februari dipersembahkan untuknya.",
      "en": "Dolya must be propitiated with gifts such as porridge, wool, thread, bread, honey and milk; literally dolya means \"share\". By the restored pagan calendar, the day of Stritennia on 1–2 February is dedicated to her.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "stricha-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {

    {
      "id": "stricha-s2",
      "url": "https://uk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A1%D1%82%D1%80%D1%96%D1%87%D0%B0",
      "title": "Стріча",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (uk)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "uk",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "stricha-c01",
      "source_id": "stricha-s1",
      "quote": "is the proto-Ukrainian goddess of good fate, one of the incarnations of Rozhanytsa, who foretells the future of married couples, the birth of children (incarnation of new souls).",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Stricha atau Dolya adalah dewi nasib baik proto-Ukraina, salah satu perwujudan Rozhanytsa, yang meramalkan masa depan pasangan suami istri dan kelahiran anak (penjelmaan jiwa-jiwa baru).",
        "en": "Stricha or Dolya is the proto-Ukrainian goddess of good fate, one of the incarnations of Rozhanytsa, who foretells the future of married couples and the birth of children (the incarnation of new souls)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stricha-c02",
      "source_id": "stricha-s1",
      "quote": "She determines the purpose of a person, therefore, at birth, she endows babies with all the virtues, predicts their future.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menentukan tujuan hidup seseorang, sehingga saat lahir ia menganugerahi bayi dengan segala kebajikan dan meramalkan masa depan mereka.",
        "en": "She determines a person's purpose, so at birth she endows babies with all the virtues and predicts their future."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stricha-c03",
      "source_id": "stricha-s2",
      "quote": "Долю завжди треба умилостивлювати принесенням їй подарунків. В пожертву Долі приносилася: каша, вовна, нитки, вино, хустки, посуд, стрічки, хліб, мед, молоко. Дослівно доля означає «частина, частка».",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dolya selalu harus ditenangkan dengan hadiah; persembahan untuknya berupa bubur, wol, benang, anggur, saputangan, peralatan dapur, pita, roti, madu, dan susu, dan secara harfiah dolya berarti \"bagian\".",
        "en": "Dolya must always be propitiated with gifts; offerings to her were porridge, wool, thread, wine, kerchiefs, dishes, ribbons, bread, honey and milk, and literally dolya means \"part, share\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stricha-c04",
      "source_id": "stricha-s2",
      "quote": "Богиня доброї долі уявляється в образі вродливої молодої жінки, яка пряде міцну рівну золоту нитку людської долі. Символами Долі є веретено, прясельце, клубок ниток.",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewi nasib baik ini dibayangkan sebagai perempuan muda jelita yang memintal benang emas nasib manusia yang kuat dan rata; lambangnya adalah kumparan, pemberat pintal, dan gulungan benang.",
        "en": "The goddess of good fate is imagined as a beautiful young woman spinning the strong, even golden thread of human fate; her symbols are the spindle, the spindle whorl and the ball of thread."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "stricha-c05",
      "source_id": "stricha-s2",
      "quote": "Долі-Стрічі присвячений день Стрітення (1-2 лютого за відновленим язичницьким календарем).",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hari Strітення (1–2 Februari menurut kalender pagan yang direkonstruksi) dipersembahkan untuk Dolya-Stricha.",
        "en": "The day of Stritennia (1–2 February by the restored pagan calendar) is dedicated to Dolya-Stricha."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en dan uk (en tampak terjemahan uk). Sebagian isi, seperti penanggalan hari raya, mengikuti kalender pagan yang direkonstruksi (neopagan) dan perlu dicek dengan kajian etnografi Ukraina."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}
```

```json
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Takhayul orang Rusia adalah kumpulan kepercayaan dan kebiasaan sehari-hari, seperti duduk sejenak sebelum bepergian atau tidak bersiul di dalam rumah.",
    "en": "The superstitions of Russians are a body of everyday beliefs and habits, such as sitting a moment before a journey or not whistling indoors.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "superstitions-of-russians-c01",
      "superstitions-of-russians-c02",
      "superstitions-of-russians-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Banyak takhayul orang Rusia menjadi bagian kehidupan sehari-hari, dan sebagian bahkan dianggap etiket sosial biasa. Pengaruhnya berbeda-beda menurut daerah dan usia. Contohnya, sebelum perjalanan jauh, pelancong dan pengantarnya duduk sejenak dalam diam sebelum meninggalkan rumah.",
      "en": "Many Russian superstitions are part of everyday life, and some are even treated as ordinary social etiquette. Their influence varies by region and age. For example, before a long journey travellers and those seeing them off sit for a moment in silence before leaving the house.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "superstitions-of-russians-c01",
        "superstitions-of-russians-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Takhayul lain melarang bersiul di dalam rumah karena mendatangkan kesulitan uang, berjabat tangan atau memberi barang di ambang pintu, dan meletakkan botol kosong di meja; botol kosong harus ditaruh di lantai. Bila diberi hewan, orang membayar uang simbolis seperti satu rubel.",
      "en": "Other superstitions forbid whistling indoors, which brings money trouble, shaking hands or handing things over on the threshold, and putting an empty bottle back on the table; it must go on the floor. When given an animal, people pay a symbolic sum such as one rouble.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "superstitions-of-russians-c03",
        "superstitions-of-russians-c04",
        "superstitions-of-russians-c05",
        "superstitions-of-russians-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "superstitions-of-russians-c01",
      "source_id": "superstitions-of-russians-s1",
      "quote": "Many superstition of Russians are staples of everyday life, and some are even considered common social etiquette despite being rooted in superstition. The influence of these traditions and superstitions varies, and their perceived importance depends on factors such as region and age.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banyak takhayul orang Rusia menjadi bagian kehidupan sehari-hari, sebagian bahkan dianggap etiket sosial biasa walaupun berakar pada takhayul; pengaruh dan pentingnya bergantung pada faktor seperti daerah dan usia.",
        "en": "Many Russian superstitions are staples of everyday life, some even treated as ordinary social etiquette despite their roots in superstition; their influence and perceived importance depend on factors such as region and age."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "superstitions-of-russians-c02",
      "source_id": "superstitions-of-russians-s1",
      "quote": "Before leaving for a long journey, travelers, and all those who are seeing them off, must sit for a moment in silence before leaving the house. It is often seen as a time to sit and think of anything one may have forgotten.",
      "locator": "Travel",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebelum berangkat ke perjalanan jauh, pelancong dan semua yang mengantarnya harus duduk sejenak dalam diam sebelum meninggalkan rumah, yang sering dianggap waktu untuk memikirkan apa saja yang mungkin terlupa.",
        "en": "Before leaving on a long journey, travellers and everyone seeing them off must sit for a moment in silence before leaving the house, often seen as a moment to think of anything forgotten."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "superstitions-of-russians-c03",
      "source_id": "superstitions-of-russians-s2",
      "quote": "Ne sifflez jamais dans un appartement, sinon vous aurez des problèmes d'argent et le propriétaire de l'appartement aussi.",
      "locator": "Coutumes",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jangan pernah bersiul di dalam rumah, karena Anda dan pemilik rumah akan kesulitan uang.",
        "en": "Never whistle indoors, or you and the owner of the flat will have money problems."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "superstitions-of-russians-c04",
      "source_id": "superstitions-of-russians-s2",
      "quote": "Ne pas serrer la main de quelqu'un ou donner quelque chose en se tenant sur le seuil de la porte.",
      "locator": "Coutumes",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jangan berjabat tangan atau memberikan sesuatu sambil berdiri di ambang pintu.",
        "en": "Do not shake hands or hand something over while standing on the threshold."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "superstitions-of-russians-c05",
      "source_id": "superstitions-of-russians-s2",
      "quote": "Dès qu'une bouteille est vide, ne la reposez pas sur la table mais sur le sol.",
      "locator": "Coutumes",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Begitu botol kosong, jangan diletakkan kembali di meja, tetapi di lantai.",
        "en": "Once a bottle is empty, do not put it back on the table but on the floor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "superstitions-of-russians-c06",
      "source_id": "superstitions-of-russians-s2",
      "quote": "Quand un animal vous est offert, il faut donner une somme d'argent symbolique, par exemple un rouble.",
      "locator": "Coutumes",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila diberi hewan, seseorang harus memberi uang simbolis, misalnya satu rubel.",
        "en": "When given an animal, one should pay a symbolic sum, for example one rouble."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
    {
      "value": "giant",

        "suur-harg-c01"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Suur härg adalah lembu raksasa gaib dalam lagu rakyat Estonia dan Finlandia, begitu besar sehingga burung layang-layang perlu sehari untuk terbang dari satu tanduknya ke tanduk lain.",
    "en": "Suur härg is a supernatural giant ox of Estonian and Finnish folk song, so huge that a swallow needs a day to fly from one horn to the other.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "suur-harg-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "\"Suur härg\" (Lembu Raksasa) adalah jenis lagu rakyat regilaul Estonia dan Finlandia tentang lembu jantan yang luar biasa besar: burung layang-layang perlu terbang seharian untuk berpindah dari satu tanduknya ke tanduk lain, dan hanya lelaki berkekuatan gaib yang dapat membunuhnya. Varian lagu menggambarkan banyaknya daging dan darah lembu itu serta benda sehari-hari atau ajaib yang dibuat darinya.",
      "en": "\"Suur härg\" (Great Ox) is a type of Estonian and Finnish regilaul folk song about a supernaturally huge ox: a swallow must fly all day to get from one horn to the other, and only a man of supernatural strength can kill it. Variants describe how much meat and blood the ox had and what everyday or wondrous things were made from it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "suur-harg-c01",
        "suur-harg-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Elias Lönnrot memasukkan motif lagu ini di awal runo ke-20 Kalevala, ketika lembu itu disembelih untuk pesta pernikahan di Pohjola. Grup musik Oort merekam salah satu variannya dan menamai album mereka \"Suur härg\".",
      "en": "Elias Lönnrot used motifs of the song at the start of the 20th runo of the Kalevala, where the ox is slaughtered for the wedding at Pohjola. The band Oort recorded one variant and named their album \"Suur härg\".",
      "claim_ids": [
        "suur-harg-c03",
        "suur-harg-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [
    {
      "id": "Mengapa lagu rakyat suka melebih-lebihkan ukuran hewan?",
      "en": "Why do folk songs like to exaggerate the size of animals?"
    }
  ],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "suur-harg-s1",
      "url": "https://et.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suur_h%C3%A4rg",
      "title": "Suur härg",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (et)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "et",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "suur-harg-c01",
      "source_id": "suur-harg-s1",
      "quote": "\"Suur härg\" on eesti ja soome regilaulutüüp, milles kirjeldatakse üleloomulikult suurt härga – näiteks pääsuke võib päeva lennata, enne kui ühelt sarvelt teisele jõuab –, kelle tapmiseks tuleb leida üleloomuliku jõuga mees.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "\"Suur härg\" (Lembu Raksasa) adalah jenis lagu rakyat regilaul Estonia dan Finlandia yang menggambarkan lembu jantan yang luar biasa besar, misalnya burung layang-layang perlu terbang seharian untuk berpindah dari satu tanduknya ke tanduk lain, dan untuk membunuhnya harus dicari lelaki berkekuatan gaib.",
        "en": "\"Suur härg\" (Great Ox) is a type of Estonian and Finnish regilaul folk song describing a supernaturally huge ox, for instance a swallow may fly a whole day before getting from one horn to the other, and to kill it a man of supernatural strength must be found."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suur-harg-c02",
      "source_id": "suur-harg-s1",
      "quote": "Laulu teisendites võib olla kirjeldatud, kui palju oli härjal liha ning verd, mida sellest tehti, milliseid tarbe- või imeasju härjast sai.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Varian lagu ini bisa menggambarkan berapa banyak daging dan darah lembu itu, apa yang dibuat darinya, dan benda sehari-hari atau benda ajaib apa yang diperoleh darinya.",
        "en": "Variants of the song may describe how much meat and blood the ox had, what was made from it and which everyday or wondrous things came from it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suur-harg-c03",
      "source_id": "suur-harg-s1",
      "quote": "Elias Lönnrot paigutas suure härja laulu motiive eepose \"Kalevala\" 20. runo algusse, kus härg tapetakse Põhjala pulma ette valmistades enne õlletegu.",
      "locator": "Ilukirjanduses ja popmuusikas",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Elias Lönnrot menempatkan motif lagu lembu raksasa di awal runo ke-20 epos Kalevala, ketika lembu itu disembelih untuk persiapan pesta pernikahan di Pohjola sebelum pembuatan bir.",
        "en": "Elias Lönnrot placed motifs of the great-ox song at the start of the 20th runo of the Kalevala, where the ox is slaughtered in preparation for the wedding at Pohjola before the beer-making."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suur-harg-c04",
      "source_id": "suur-harg-s1",
      "quote": "Ansambel Oort on laulu ühe variandi töötlusena lindistanud ja selle järgi nimetanud oma plaadi \"Suur härg\".",
      "locator": "Ilukirjanduses ja popmuusikas",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grup musik Oort merekam aransemen salah satu varian lagu ini dan menamai albumnya \"Suur härg\".",
        "en": "The band Oort recorded an arrangement of one variant of the song and named their album \"Suur härg\" after it."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  ]
}
```

```json
    "id": "Syöjätär adalah demon perempuan pemakan manusia dalam puisi rakyat dan dongeng Finlandia, yang dianggap asal-usul ular dan berbagai hal buruk di dunia.",
    "en": "Syöjätär is a man-eating female demon of Finnish folk poetry and fairy tales, held to be the origin of snakes and various evils in the world.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam puisi rakyat dan dongeng, Syöjätär adalah makhluk perempuan jahat, roh jahat dalam tradisi rakyat Finlandia. Pada 1789 Christfried Ganander menggambarkannya sebagai demon perempuan, setengah demon atau setengah troll, pemakan daging yang kejam dan kanibal. Dalam kumpulan mantra susunan Elias Lönnrot ia roh jahat atau penyihir yang disebut bersama Hiisi, Perkele, dan Louhi. Sebuah lagu menyebut ia lahir ketika Louhi melahirkan Sembilan Penyakit dan anak kesepuluh, seorang putri.",
      "en": "In folk poetry and fairy tales Syöjätär is an evil female being, an evil spirit of Finnish folk tradition. In 1789 Christfried Ganander described her as a she-demon, part demon or part troll, a savage eater of flesh and of people. In the charm collection compiled by Elias Lönnrot she is an evil spirit or witch named alongside Hiisi, Perkele and Louhi. One song says she was born when Louhi bore the Nine Diseases and a tenth child, a daughter.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "syojatar-c01",
        "syojatar-c05",
        "syojatar-c08",
        "syojatar-c09",
        "syojatar-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Syöjätär dianggap merusak ciptaan yang baik: kadal disebut brosnya, ular lahir dari ludahnya, dan karena keluhannya Tuhan mengurangi puting sapi sehingga susu berkurang. Ia dibandingkan dengan Áhčešeatni Sami, dan dalam dongeng ia sering dibunuh pada akhir cerita, dengan ular dan hewan tak menyenangkan muncul dari jasadnya.",
      "en": "Syöjätär is held to spoil the good creation: the lizard is called her brooch, the snake was born of her spit, and because of her complaint God reduced the cow's teats so there is less milk. She is compared to the Sámi Áhčešeatni, and in fairy tales she is often killed at the end, with snakes and unpleasant animals emerging from her corpse.",

    {
      "id": "Dalam lagu \"Putri Raja dan Syöjätär\", ia menyihir seorang putri menjadi syöjätär yang buta dan bisu lalu menyamar sebagai sang putri. Sang putri akhirnya kembali ke wujudnya, dan bersama saudaranya memancing Syöjätär ke sauna, tempat ia jatuh ke lubang jebakan dan terbakar.",
      "en": "In the song \"King's Princess and Syöjätär\" she bewitches a princess into a blind, mute syöjätär and takes on the princess's likeness. The princess finally regains her form and, with her brother, lures Syöjätär into a sauna, where she falls into a trap pit and is burned.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c01",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s1",
      "quote": "In 1789, Christfried Ganander described Syöjätär in Mythologia Fennica as a female demon, half-demon or half troll, a cruel flesh-eater and a cannibal (\"eater of men\").",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1789 Christfried Ganander dalam Mythologia Fennica menggambarkan Syöjätär sebagai demon perempuan, setengah demon atau setengah troll, pemakan daging yang kejam dan kanibal (\"pemakan manusia\").",
        "en": "In 1789 Christfried Ganander described Syöjätär in Mythologia Fennica as a female demon, half-demon or half-troll, a cruel flesh-eater and a cannibal (\"eater of men\")."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c02",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s1",
      "quote": "The lizard is called the brooch of Syöjätär, and the snake was born out of Syöjätär's spit.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadal disebut bros Syöjätär, dan ular lahir dari ludah Syöjätär.",
        "en": "The lizard is called Syöjätär's brooch, and the snake was born of Syöjätär's spit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c03",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s1",
      "quote": "Syöjätär could be seen as a memory of those myths and has been compared to Sámi Áhčešeatni, whose corpse turns into frogs, toads, and other unpleasant animals. In fairytales involving Syöjätär, she is often killed at the end, and snakes and other unpleasant animals come from her corpse.",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Syöjätär bisa dilihat sebagai kenangan mitos dualisme pencipta baik dan buruk dalam mitos asal-usul Fino-Ugria, dan dibandingkan dengan Áhčešeatni Sami yang jasadnya menjadi katak, kodok, dan hewan tak menyenangkan lain; dalam dongeng ia sering dibunuh pada akhir cerita dan dari jasadnya muncul ular dan hewan tak menyenangkan lainnya.",
        "en": "Syöjätär may be a memory of the good-and-bad-creator dualism of Finno-Ugric origin myths and has been compared to the Sámi Áhčešeatni, whose corpse turns into frogs, toads and other unpleasant animals; in fairy tales she is often killed at the end and snakes and other unpleasant animals come from her corpse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c04",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s1",
      "quote": "Cows used to have their stomachs full of teats, but as Syöjätär complained about having to milk so much, God reduced the number of them, also resulting in less available milk.",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dahulu perut sapi penuh puting, tetapi karena Syöjätär mengeluh harus memerah terlalu banyak, Tuhan mengurangi jumlahnya sehingga susu pun berkurang.",
        "en": "Cows used to have bellies full of teats, but because Syöjätär complained of having to milk so much, God reduced their number, leaving less milk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c05",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s1",
      "quote": "One song also describes the origin of Syöjätär herself: she came to be when Louhi gave birth to the Nine diseases and a tenth child, a daughter.",
      "locator": "Runic songs",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah lagu menceritakan asal-usul Syöjätär sendiri: ia lahir ketika Louhi melahirkan Sembilan Penyakit dan anak kesepuluh, seorang putri.",
        "en": "One song tells of Syöjätär's own origin: she came to be when Louhi gave birth to the Nine Diseases and a tenth child, a daughter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c06",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s1",
      "quote": "In the song Kuninkaan prinsessa ja Syöjätär 'King's Princess and Syöjätär', Syöjätär casts a spell, turning a princess into a blind and mute syöjätär, and herself to look like the princess.",
      "locator": "Runic songs",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam lagu \"Kuninkaan prinsessa ja Syöjätär\" (Putri Raja dan Syöjätär), Syöjätär menyihir seorang putri menjadi syöjätär yang buta dan bisu, dan mengubah dirinya agar tampak seperti sang putri.",
        "en": "In the song \"Kuninkaan prinsessa ja Syöjätär\" (King's Princess and Syöjätär), Syöjätär casts a spell turning a princess into a blind, mute syöjätär and herself into the princess's likeness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c07",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s1",
      "quote": "The princess got her own form back and the siblings warm up a sauna where they lure Syöjätär into. Syöjätär falls into a deep trap pit and the sauna is set on fire, burning her into the pit.",
      "locator": "Runic songs",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sang putri kembali ke wujud aslinya, lalu ia dan saudaranya memanaskan sauna dan memancing Syöjätär masuk; Syöjätär jatuh ke lubang jebakan dalam dan sauna dibakar sehingga ia terbakar di lubang itu.",
        "en": "The princess regains her form, and she and her brother heat a sauna and lure Syöjätär inside; Syöjätär falls into a deep trap pit and the sauna is set alight, burning her in the pit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c08",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s2",
      "quote": "Syöjätär on kansanrunoudessa ja saduissa paha naisolento. Syöjätär voi tarkoittaa myös viettelijätärtä, miestennielijää.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam puisi rakyat dan dongeng, Syöjätär adalah makhluk perempuan jahat; kata ini juga bisa berarti penggoda, perempuan \"penelan lelaki\".",
        "en": "In folk poetry and fairy tales Syöjätär is an evil female being; the word can also mean a seductress, a \"man-eater\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c09",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s2",
      "quote": "Suomalaisessa mytologiassa ja kansanperinteessä syöjätär on paha henki.",
      "locator": "Syöjätär suomalaisessa perinteessä",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi dan tradisi rakyat Finlandia, syöjätär adalah roh jahat.",
        "en": "In Finnish mythology and folk tradition the syöjätär is an evil spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "syojatar-c10",
      "source_id": "syojatar-s2",
      "quote": "Elias Lönnrotin kokoamassa teoksessa Suomen kansan muinaisia loitsurunoja syöjätär on paha henki tai noita, jota voidaan käyttää manattaessa. Syöjätär mainitaan muiden pahojen henkien, kuten Hiiden, Perkeleen ja Louhen kanssa.",
      "locator": "Syöjätär suomalaisessa perinteessä",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kumpulan Suomen kansan muinaisia loitsurunoja susunan Elias Lönnrot, syöjätär adalah roh jahat atau penyihir yang bisa dipakai dalam mantra pengusiran, dan disebut bersama roh jahat lain seperti Hiisi, Perkele, dan Louhi.",
        "en": "In Elias Lönnrot's collection Suomen kansan muinaisia loitsurunoja, syöjätär is an evil spirit or witch who can be used in conjurations, mentioned alongside other evil spirits such as Hiisi, Perkele and Louhi."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Uriaș adalah sebutan Rumania untuk raksasa dalam cerita rakyat, makhluk berwajah manusia yang begitu besar hingga bisa mengobrol dari satu gunung ke gunung lain.",
    "en": "Uriaș is the Romanian name for the giants of folklore, human-faced beings so huge they can chat from one mountain to another.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "urias-c01",
      "urias-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Uriaș adalah sebutan umum dalam bahasa Rumania untuk raksasa, tokoh penting dalam cerita rakyat Rumania. Mereka berwajah manusia, kekar dan perkasa, setinggi beberapa depa, dengan langkah sangat jauh dan kepala sangat besar; mereka bisa mengobrol dari satu gunung ke gunung lain dan berpegangan pada pegangan langit. Kadang mereka digambarkan sebagai kiklops bermata satu pemakan manusia.",
      "en": "Uriaș is the common Romanian word for giants, prominent figures of Romanian folklore. They have human faces, are brawny and mighty, several fathoms tall, with enormous strides and huge heads; they can chat from one mountain to another and grab the handles of the sky. Sometimes they are described as one-eyed, man-eating cyclopes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "urias-c01",
        "urias-c02",
        "urias-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebuah kisah umum menceritakan anak uriaș yang membawa pulang seorang petani beserta bajak dan lembunya seperti mainan, lalu orang tuanya meramalkan punahnya bangsa raksasa yang akan digantikan makhluk kecil. Di beberapa daerah Oltenia dan Transilvania mereka disebut Jidovi atau Novaci, dan mereka dianggap membangun benteng prasejarah dan gundukan tanah, yang sebenarnya tumulus pemakaman Zaman Perunggu dan Besi.",
      "en": "A common tale tells of an uriaș child who brings home a farmer with his plough and oxen like toys, and the parents foretell the passing of the giants and their replacement by small beings. In parts of Oltenia and Transylvania they are called Jidovi or Novaci, and they are credited with building prehistoric forts and earth mounds, which are in fact burial tumuli of the Bronze and Iron Ages.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "urias-c04",
        "urias-c05",
        "urias-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

        "id": "Uriaș adalah sebutan umum dalam bahasa Rumania untuk raksasa, tokoh penting dalam cerita rakyat Rumania.",

    {
      "id": "urias-c02",
      "source_id": "urias-s2",
      "quote": "Uriașii sunt figuri de mitologie sau basm, cu chip de om, mari, vânjoși, voinici, înalți de mai mulți stânjeni, cu pasul de o de poștă, cu capul cât trei ocale domnești sau mare cât o baniță. Ei pot sta la taifas de la un munte la altul, fiind în stare să se apuce cu mâinile de toartele cerului.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uriaș adalah tokoh mitologi atau dongeng berwajah manusia, besar, kekar, dan perkasa, setinggi beberapa depa, dengan langkah sejauh satu pos perjalanan dan kepala sebesar takaran gandum; mereka bisa mengobrol dari satu gunung ke gunung lain dan mampu berpegangan pada pegangan langit.",
        "en": "The uriași are figures of myth or fairy tale with human faces, huge, brawny and strong, several fathoms tall, with a stride as long as a post-stage and heads as big as a grain measure; they can chat from one mountain to another and can grab the handles of the sky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "urias-c03",
      "source_id": "urias-s2",
      "quote": "Uneori sunt descriși ca ciclopi, cu un singur ochi în frunte și hrănindu-se cu carne de om.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang mereka digambarkan sebagai kiklops bermata satu di dahi yang memakan daging manusia.",
        "en": "Sometimes they are described as cyclopes with a single eye in the forehead, feeding on human flesh."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "urias-c04",
      "source_id": "urias-s2",
      "quote": "De asemenea, e frecvent tipul de poveste în care copilul de Uriaș care aduce acasă în palmă (sau poală) un om cu plugul cu boi, ca pe niște jucării, iar părinții îi explică divinatoriu pieirea propriei stirpe și înlocuirea ei cu ființe mărunte. În unele zone din Oltenia și Transilvania Uriașii se mai numesc și Jidovi sau Novaci.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sering ada kisah anak uriaș yang membawa pulang seorang manusia beserta bajak dan lembunya di telapak tangan seperti mainan, lalu orang tuanya meramalkan punahnya bangsa mereka yang akan digantikan makhluk-makhluk kecil; di beberapa daerah Oltenia dan Transilvania, uriaș juga disebut Jidovi atau Novaci.",
        "en": "A common tale tells of an uriaș child who brings home a man with his plough and oxen in his palm like toys, and the parents prophetically explain the passing of their own kind and its replacement by tiny beings; in parts of Oltenia and Transylvania the uriași are also called Jidovi or Novaci."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "urias-c05",
      "source_id": "urias-s2",
      "quote": "Uriașii sunt cunoscuți și ca giganți care ar fi construit cetățile preistorice și movilele de pământ.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uriaș juga dikenal sebagai raksasa yang konon membangun benteng-benteng prasejarah dan gundukan tanah.",
        "en": "The uriași are also known as giants said to have built prehistoric forts and earth mounds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "urias-c06",
      "source_id": "urias-s2",
      "quote": "În ținuturile extracarpatice movilele puse pe seama Uriașilor, care ar fi cărat pământul cu poala, sunt de origine antropomorfă, tumuli funerari din Epocile Bronzului și Fierului.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di daerah di luar Karpatia, gundukan yang dikaitkan dengan uriaș, yang konon mengangkut tanah dengan pangkuannya, sebenarnya buatan manusia, yaitu tumulus pemakaman dari Zaman Perunggu dan Besi.",
        "en": "In the lands beyond the Carpathians, the mounds attributed to the uriași, said to have carried earth in their laps, are man-made, burial tumuli of the Bronze and Iron Ages."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ved adalah makhluk raksasa berbulu dalam kepercayaan wilayah Bilogora, Kroasia utara; vedi baik membantu rumah tangga, sedangkan vedi hutan menjauhi manusia.",
    "en": "The ved is a hairy giant of the folk belief of the Bilogora region, northern Croatia; good vedi helped households while forest vedi shunned people.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ved-mythology-c01",
      "ved-mythology-c03",
      "ved-mythology-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ved adalah makhluk mitologis yang tercatat di wilayah Bilogora, Kroasia utara, terutama di kalangan penutur dialek Kajkavia. Vedi digambarkan sebagai makhluk lelaki mirip manusia setinggi rumah petani dan berbulu lebat. Mereka sangat kuat, mampu mencabut pohon, dan tiupan dada mereka yang besar dapat menimbulkan badai.",
      "en": "The ved is a mythological being recorded in the Bilogora region of northern Croatia, mainly among speakers of the Kajkavian dialect. The vedi were described as male humanlike beings as tall as a peasant house and covered in hair. They were very strong, able to uproot trees, and the blowing of their great chests could raise storms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ved-mythology-c01",
        "ved-mythology-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ada vedi baik dan buruk. Vedi hutan menjauhi manusia dan kadang menjadikan pemuda yang tersesat sebagai budak untuk sementara. Vedi baik membantu pekerjaan sehari-hari; setiap rumah tangga punya ved sendiri yang begitu setia sampai mencelakai rumah tangga lain. Saat bencana orang berdoa agar vedi mereka menolong. Kisah terakhir tentang kunjungan vedi berasal dari pertengahan abad ke-19.",
      "en": "There were good and bad vedi. Forest vedi shunned people and sometimes kept a lost young man as a slave for a while. Good vedi helped with daily work; each household had its own ved, so devoted that it harmed other households. In times of disaster people prayed for their vedi to help. The last accounts of vedi visits date from the mid-19th century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ved-mythology-c03",
        "ved-mythology-c04",
        "ved-mythology-c05",
        "ved-mythology-c06"
      ]

    {
      "id": "ved-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "ved-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "A ved is a mythological being recorded in the region of Bilogora in northern Croatia, predominantly among the region's speakers of the Kajkavian dialect. The vedi (plural) were described as male human-like creatures as high as a peasant house, completely covered with hair.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ved adalah makhluk mitologis yang tercatat di wilayah Bilogora, Kroasia utara, terutama di kalangan penutur dialek Kajkavia; vedi (bentuk jamak) digambarkan sebagai makhluk lelaki mirip manusia setinggi rumah petani yang seluruh tubuhnya berbulu.",
        "en": "A ved is a mythological being recorded in the Bilogora region of northern Croatia, chiefly among speakers of the Kajkavian dialect; the vedi (plural) were described as male humanlike creatures as tall as a peasant house, covered in hair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "ved-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "They were very strong, able to uproot trees and carry heavy loads, while their chest was so large that they could make storms by blowing. When they spoke or sang, it was heard far away.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka sangat kuat, mampu mencabut pohon dan memikul beban berat, dan dada mereka begitu besar sehingga tiupannya bisa menimbulkan badai; suara mereka saat berbicara atau bernyanyi terdengar sampai jauh.",
        "en": "They were very strong, able to uproot trees and carry heavy loads, with chests so large they could raise storms by blowing; when they spoke or sang they were heard far away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "ved-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "There were good and bad vedi. The latter did not associate with people and stayed in their forests, because of which they were also called the forest vedi. They normally did no harm to people, but if a bad ved encountered a young man in a forest, he would take him to his town and keep him as a slave for some time.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada vedi baik dan buruk; vedi buruk tidak bergaul dengan manusia dan tinggal di hutan, sehingga disebut vedi hutan; biasanya mereka tak mencelakai orang, tetapi bila bertemu pemuda di hutan, ved buruk akan membawanya ke kotanya dan menjadikannya budak untuk sementara.",
        "en": "There were good and bad vedi; the bad ones shunned people and stayed in their forests, hence the name forest vedi; normally harmless, but a bad ved meeting a young man in the forest would take him to its town and keep him as a slave for a while."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "ved-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The good vedi visited people and helped them in their everyday work or troubles. Each household had its own ved who was very devoted to it, often to the extent that he did harm to other households and their vedi.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vedi baik mengunjungi manusia dan membantu pekerjaan sehari-hari atau kesulitan mereka; setiap rumah tangga punya ved sendiri yang sangat setia, sering sampai mencelakai rumah tangga lain beserta vedi mereka.",
        "en": "The good vedi visited people and helped with daily work or troubles; each household had its own ved, so devoted that it often harmed other households and their vedi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "ved-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "If people expected a flood, storm, or other calamity, they would pray, \"Grant, Oh God, that our vedi help us!\" or \"Dear God, grant that our vedi help us, and that their vedi do no harm to us!\" Stories have it that after such prayers the vedi would quickly come to rescue.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila menghadapi banjir, badai, atau bencana lain, orang berdoa agar Tuhan mengizinkan vedi mereka menolong dan vedi orang lain tidak mencelakai; menurut cerita, vedi segera datang menolong setelah doa itu.",
        "en": "Facing a flood, storm or other calamity, people prayed that God would let their vedi help them and others' vedi do them no harm; stories say the vedi then came quickly to the rescue."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "ved-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Last accounts of vedi visiting people date from the mid 19th century. In the end only certain individuals were allegedly able to see them.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah terakhir tentang vedi yang mengunjungi manusia berasal dari pertengahan abad ke-19, dan pada akhirnya konon hanya orang tertentu yang bisa melihat mereka.",
        "en": "The last accounts of vedi visiting people date from the mid-19th century, and in the end only certain individuals could supposedly see them."
      }
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vitore adalah ular ilahi penjaga rumah dalam mitologi Albania, terkait dengan nasib, keberuntungan, jiwa leluhur, dan pemujaan perapian.",
    "en": "Vitore is the divine household serpent of Albanian mythology, linked with fate, good fortune, the ancestors' souls and the cult of the hearth.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vitore-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Albania, Vitore adalah ular ilahi penjaga rumah yang terkait dengan nasib manusia, keberuntungan, dan jiwa para leluhur. Ia erat dengan pemujaan perapian dan terutama dianggap pelindung keluarga dan rumah. Namanya ditafsirkan berasal dari kata untuk alat tenun, sehingga berarti \"perempuan pemintal\", terkait dengan menenun nasib.",
      "en": "In Albanian mythology and folklore Vitore is a divine household serpent linked with human destiny, good fortune and the souls of the ancestors. She is closely tied to the cult of the hearth and is above all the guardian of family and house. Her name is interpreted as coming from the word for a loom, meaning \"a woman who spins\", linked to the weaving of fate.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vitore-c01",
        "vitore-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Vitorja biasanya digambarkan sebagai ular kecil berwarna-warni bertanduk emas yang baik hati, tetapi dalam sebagian tradisi sebagai perempuan tua. Bila ia tinggal di dinding rumah, ia membawa keberuntungan dan dihormati penghuni, dan desisannya menandakan peristiwa penting keluarga. Ular adalah totem hewan orang Albania, dan siluet ular diukir di dinding dan pintu rumah untuk keberuntungan.",
      "en": "Vitorja is usually described as a small, colourful, benign golden-horned serpent, but in some traditions as an old woman. When she lives in a house's walls she brings luck and is respected by the household, and her whispering announces important family events. The serpent is an animal totem of the Albanians, and snake silhouettes are carved on walls and doors for good fortune.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vitore-c03",
        "vitore-c04",
        "vitore-c05",
        "vitore-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Vitore mencerminkan kepercayaan bahwa jiwa leluhur menjelma dalam ular rumah dan terkait pemujaan perapian; siluet ular diukir di rumah sebagai penangkal mata jahat.",
    "en": "Vitore reflects the belief that ancestral souls take form in household serpents, tied to the cult of the hearth; snake silhouettes are carved on houses against the evil eye.",

    {
      "id": "vitore-c01",
      "source_id": "vitore-s1",
      "quote": "is a household divine serpent (gjarpër or bollë) in Albanian mythology and folklore, associated with human destiny, good fortune and the souls of the ancestors. In folk beliefs the household serpent is strongly related to the cult of the hearth (vatër) and it is especially considered the guardian of the family and the house.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vitore adalah ular ilahi penjaga rumah dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Albania, terkait dengan nasib manusia, keberuntungan, dan jiwa para leluhur; dalam kepercayaan rakyat ular rumah ini erat dengan pemujaan perapian (vatër) dan terutama dianggap pelindung keluarga dan rumah.",
        "en": "Vitore is a household divine serpent of Albanian mythology and folklore, linked with human destiny, good fortune and the souls of the ancestors; in folk belief the household serpent is closely tied to the cult of the hearth (vatër) and is above all the guardian of the family and house."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vitore-c02",
      "source_id": "vitore-s1",
      "quote": "The name Vitore has been analyzed as descendant from the Albanian vejtare > vektare > vek /vegj , \"loom\", thus meaning \"a spinster, a woman who spins\", related to the weaving of fate.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Vitore ditafsirkan berasal dari kata Albania vejtare > vektare > vek/vegj, \"alat tenun\", sehingga berarti \"perempuan pemintal\", terkait dengan menenun nasib.",
        "en": "The name Vitore has been analysed as descending from Albanian vejtare > vektare > vek/vegj, \"loom\", thus meaning \"a woman who spins\", linked to the weaving of fate."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vitore-c03",
      "source_id": "vitore-s1",
      "quote": "The serpent is an animal totem of the Albanians, especially in the north. The belief of the personification of the souls of the ancestors in household serpents has been handed down from prehistoric times.",
      "locator": "Background",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular adalah totem hewan orang Albania, terutama di utara, dan kepercayaan bahwa jiwa leluhur menjelma dalam ular rumah diwariskan sejak zaman prasejarah.",
        "en": "The serpent is an animal totem of the Albanians, especially in the north, and the belief that ancestral souls are personified in household serpents has come down from prehistoric times."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vitore-c04",
      "source_id": "vitore-s1",
      "quote": "Vitorja is usually described as a small, colourful and benign golden horned serpent. However, in some traditions Vitorja is described also as an old woman, a mythological figure similar to Nëna e Vatrës. Vitorja is also identified with Fatia in southern folklore and Ora in central and northern folklore.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vitorja biasanya digambarkan sebagai ular kecil berwarna-warni yang baik hati dan bertanduk emas, tetapi dalam sebagian tradisi juga sebagai perempuan tua mirip Nëna e Vatrës; ia juga disamakan dengan Fatia dalam folklor selatan dan Ora dalam folklor tengah dan utara.",
        "en": "Vitorja is usually described as a small, colourful, benign golden-horned serpent, but in some traditions also as an old woman similar to Nëna e Vatrës; she is also identified with Fatia in southern folklore and Ora in central and northern folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vitore-c05",
      "source_id": "vitore-s1",
      "quote": "The cult of the household serpent is implemented in the Albanian traditional art: the snake silouhette commonly appears carved on the walls and doors of Albanian houses for good fortune and to ward off the evil eye.",
      "locator": "image caption",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan ular rumah tampak dalam seni tradisional Albania: siluet ular sering diukir di dinding dan pintu rumah untuk keberuntungan dan penangkal mata jahat.",
        "en": "The household serpent cult appears in Albanian traditional art: a snake silhouette is often carved on house walls and doors for good fortune and against the evil eye."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vitore-c06",
      "source_id": "vitore-s2",
      "quote": "Si gjarpër, kur Vitorja banon në muret e një shtëpie, besohet se sjell fat, kështu që banorët e trajtojnë me shumë respekt. Pëshpëritja e saj lajmëron ngjarje të rëndësishme për familjen.",
      "locator": "Besimet",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai ular, bila Vitorja tinggal di dinding rumah ia dipercaya membawa keberuntungan sehingga penghuni memperlakukannya dengan sangat hormat, dan desisannya menandakan peristiwa penting bagi keluarga.",
        "en": "As a serpent, when Vitorja lives in a house's walls she is believed to bring luck, so the household treats her with great respect, and her whispering announces important family events."
      }
    }
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Valasen adalah roh rumah berbulu lebat dalam mitologi Belarus yang pada malam hari mengganggu orang tidur dengan cakar tujuh jarinya.",
    "en": "Valasen is a shaggy house spirit of Belarusian mythology that torments sleepers at night with its seven-fingered claws.",
    "claim_ids": [

      "wd-q110202710-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Valasen adalah makhluk demonik dari mitologi Belarus yang hanya dikenal di distrik Vileyka, Oblast Minsk. Ia dibayangkan sebagai roh rumah kecil buruk rupa berbulu gelap lebat, bertelapak tangan tujuh jari dengan kuku panjang seperti cakar, dan berjalan tegak seperti manusia.",
      "en": "Valasen is a demonic being of Belarusian mythology known only in the Vileyka district of Minsk Oblast. It is imagined as a small, ugly house spirit with thick dark fur, seven-fingered hands with long claw-like nails, walking upright like a person.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q110202710-c01",
        "wd-q110202710-c02",
        "wd-q110202710-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Setiap malam ia mengganggu orang yang tidur: mencakar lelaki hingga berdarah dengan luka yang lama sembuh, menakuti anak-anak dengan desisan dan gelitikan di telapak kaki, dan meraba perempuan. Di tempat yang ia sentuh tumbuh rambut. Perilakunya mirip Domovik dan makhluk penakut orang tidur seperti Mara.",
      "en": "Every night it torments sleepers: scratching men until they bleed with slow-healing wounds, frightening children with hissing and foot-tickling, and groping women. Hair grows wherever it touches. Its behaviour resembles the Domovik and sleep-terrifying beings such as Mara.",

      ]
```
