  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q110202710-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q110202710-s1",
      "quote": "Włosień (biał. \"Валасень\") – istota demoniczna z mitologii białoruskiej, zlokalizowana tylko w rejonie wilejskim obwodu mińskiego.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Valasen (bahasa Polandia: Włosień) adalah makhluk demonik dari mitologi Belarus yang hanya dikenal di distrik Vileyka, Oblast Minsk.",
        "en": "Valasen (Polish Włosień) is a demonic being of Belarusian mythology found only in the Vileyka district of Minsk Oblast."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q110202710-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q110202710-s1",
      "quote": "To mały, kudłaty demon domowy z wielkimi, siedmiopalczastymi łapami zakończonymi pazurami. Nocami szturchał i drapał do krwi śpiących mężczyzn. Rany zadane przez włosienia długo się goiły i ropiały.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia demon rumah kecil berbulu lebat dengan cakar besar bertujuh jari; pada malam hari ia menyodok dan mencakar lelaki yang tidur hingga berdarah, dan luka yang ia buat lama sembuh serta bernanah.",
        "en": "It is a small shaggy household demon with large seven-fingered clawed paws; at night it poked and scratched sleeping men until they bled, and the wounds it made healed slowly and festered."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q110202710-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q110202710-s1",
      "quote": "Straszył też dzieci, sycząc im do ucha i łaskocząc je w podeszwy stóp, a kobiety obmacywał podczas snu. W miejscach przez niego dotkniętych wyrastały włosy.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga menakuti anak-anak dengan mendesis di telinga dan menggelitik telapak kaki mereka, meraba perempuan yang sedang tidur, dan di tempat yang ia sentuh tumbuh rambut.",
        "en": "It also frightened children by hissing in their ears and tickling the soles of their feet, groped women in their sleep, and hair grew wherever it touched."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q110202710-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q110202710-s2",
      "quote": "Валасень — у народных павер’ях хатні дух які па начах трывожіць і пужае людзей у сне. У сваіх паводзінах ён нагадвае Дамавіка, а таксама тых міфічных істот, якія пужаюць людзей у сне. Напрыклад Мара ці Начніца.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat, Valasen adalah roh rumah yang setiap malam mengganggu dan menakuti orang yang tidur; perilakunya mirip Domovik dan makhluk mitos penakut orang tidur seperti Mara atau Nachnitsa.",
        "en": "In folk belief Valasen is a house spirit that disturbs and frightens people in their sleep at night; its behaviour resembles the Domovik and other sleep-terrifying beings such as Mara or Nachnitsa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q110202710-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q110202710-s2",
      "quote": "Валасень уяўляецца невялікай пачварнай істотай, парослай густой і доўгай цёмнай поўсцю. Ён таксама мае сяміпалыя далоні і доўгія пазногці-кіпцюры. Ходзіць Валасень, як і людзі, выпрастаўшыся.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Valasen dibayangkan sebagai makhluk kecil buruk rupa berbulu gelap yang lebat dan panjang, bertelapak tangan tujuh jari dengan kuku panjang seperti cakar, dan berjalan tegak seperti manusia.",
        "en": "Valasen is imagined as a small ugly creature covered in thick, long dark fur, with seven-fingered hands and long claw-like nails, walking upright like a person."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "id": "Kaduk adalah roh rawa jahat dalam mitologi Belarus, personifikasi epilepsi, yang memerintah roh-roh kecil dari rawa paling becek di hutan tergelap.",
    "en": "Kaduk is an evil marsh spirit of Belarusian mythology, the personification of epilepsy, ruling lesser spirits from the boggiest marsh in the darkest forest.",

      "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Kaduk adalah roh rawa dan salah satu roh jahat utama, personifikasi penyakit epilepsi; namanya berasal dari kata Latin caducus, \"yang jatuh\". Ia tinggal di rawa paling becek di hutan paling gelap, memerintah roh-roh najis kecil dan mengutus mereka mencelakai manusia.",
      "en": "In Belarusian mythology Kaduk is a marsh spirit and one of the chief evil spirits, the personification of epilepsy; its name comes from Latin caducus, \"falling\". It lives in the boggiest marsh of the darkest forest, ruling lesser unclean spirits and sending them to harm people.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17047430-c01",
        "wd-q17047430-c02",

      "id": "Menurut kepercayaan, Kaduk memakan anak-anak kecil yang dibawakan roh bawahannya. Dalam satu gambaran ia binatang berkepala besar berbulu dengan kerongkongan lebar, gigi putih, dan lidah merah menyala, siap menelan manusia bulat-bulat.       "title": "Кадук",
      "author": null,

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047430-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047430-s1",
      "quote": "Кадук — балотны дух; у беларускай міфалогіі адзін з галоўных злых духаў, персаніфікацыя хваробы эпілепсіі.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaduk adalah roh rawa, salah satu roh jahat utama dalam mitologi Belarus dan personifikasi penyakit epilepsi.",
        "en": "Kaduk is a marsh spirit, one of the chief evil spirits of Belarusian mythology and the personification of epilepsy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047430-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047430-s1",
      "quote": "Сваё імя атрымаў ад лацінскай назвы хваробы caducus — «падучы».",
      "locator": "Этымалогія імя",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berasal dari nama Latin penyakit itu, caducus, \"yang jatuh\".",
        "en": "Its name comes from the Latin name of the disease, caducus, \"falling\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047430-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047430-s1",
      "quote": "Жыве ў самым цёмным лесе, да таго ж у самым багністым балоце. Тут у яго на службе знаходзяцца меншыя нячысцікі. Тут Кадук чыніць над імі расправу; адсюль жа ён пасылае іх шкодзіць людзям.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tinggal di hutan paling gelap, di rawa paling becek, dengan roh-roh najis yang lebih kecil sebagai pelayannya; di sana ia menghakimi mereka dan dari sana ia mengutus mereka mencelakai manusia.",
        "en": "It lives in the darkest forest, in the boggiest marsh, served by lesser unclean spirits; there it judges them and from there sends them to harm people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047430-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047430-s1",
      "quote": "Беларусы вераць, што Кадук есць маленькіх дзяцей, якіх яму носяць падначаленыя духі, чым прыводзіць той ці іншы род да вымарачнасці.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Belarus percaya Kaduk memakan anak-anak kecil yang dibawakan roh-roh bawahannya, sehingga suatu keluarga bisa punah.",
        "en": "Belarusians believe Kaduk eats small children brought to it by its subordinate spirits, driving whole families to extinction."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047430-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047430-s1",
      "quote": "Па іншых звестках Кадук — звер з вялізарнай калматай галавой і шырокім горлам аж да самых вушэй. У яго пашчы блішчаць белыя зубы і чырванее, як агонь, язык, і здаецца, што ён гатовы праглынуць чалавека цалкам, з касцямі і патрахамі.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut keterangan lain, Kaduk adalah binatang berkepala besar berbulu lebat dengan kerongkongan lebar sampai ke telinga, gigi putih berkilat, dan lidah merah menyala, seolah siap menelan manusia bulat-bulat.",
        "en": "By other accounts Kaduk is a beast with a huge shaggy head and a throat wide right up to its ears, gleaming white teeth and a fire-red tongue, seemingly ready to swallow a person whole."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047430-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047430-s1",
      "quote": "Калі б не гэтыя абмежаванні, то Кадук, лічылі беларусы, даўно пахапаў бы ўсіх людзей. І, калі нехта менавіта ў такі ліхі момант пажадае, каб іншага чалавека ўзяў Кадук, то праклён здзесніцца.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Belarus menganggap bahwa tanpa batasan waktu kemunculannya, Kaduk sudah lama menculik semua orang; dan bila seseorang mengutuk orang lain agar diambil Kaduk tepat pada saat sial itu, kutukan itu akan terwujud.",
        "en": "Belarusians held that without the limits on when it can appear, Kaduk would long since have seized everyone; and if someone wishes, at just such an evil moment, that Kaduk take another person, the curse will come true."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17047430-c07",
      "source_id": "wd-q17047430-s2",
      "quote": "найстаріший з лісових духів та демонів що живе в глибині самого темного лісу в в'язкому болоті.",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Ukraina menyebut Kaduk sebagai roh dan demon hutan tertua yang tinggal di kedalaman hutan paling gelap di rawa lengket.",
        "en": "The Ukrainian article calls Kaduk the oldest of the forest spirits and demons, living deep in the darkest forest in a sticky bog."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Khabyor adalah raja udang karang dalam folklor Belarus, bercapit dan bermata emas, yang membantu orang menemukan barang yang hilang di air.",
    "en": "Khabyor is the king of crayfish in Belarusian folklore, golden-clawed and golden-eyed, who helps people find things lost in the water.",

      "wd-q17116414-c03"

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Belarus, Khabyor adalah raja semua udang karang yang dikenali dari capit dan matanya yang keemasan. Ia juga disebut roh danau, udang karang tertua, pelindung semua krustasea, dan penjaga kerajaan danau. Dalam dongeng ajaib ia penolong gaib sang pahlawan, memerintahkan udang-udang karang mencari cincin yang hilang di laut, dan ia juga membantu orang menemukan barang yang hilang di air.",
      "en": "In Belarusian folklore Khabyor is the king of all crayfish, known by his golden claws and eyes. He is also called a lake spirit, the oldest crayfish, patron of all crustaceans and protector of the lake realm. In magic tales he is the hero's magical helper, ordering the crayfish to find a ring lost in the sea, and he helps people find things lost in the water.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17116414-c01",

        "wd-q17116414-c03"

      "id": "wd-q17116414-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q17116414-s1",
      "quote": "Хабёр — у беларускім фальклоры цар усіх ракаў, якога можна было пазнаць па залатых клюшнях і вачах.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",

      "id": "wd-q17116414-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17116414-s1",
      "quote": "У чарадзейных казках — чароўны памочнік героя, які загадвае ракам знайсці ў моры згублены пярсцёнак.",

      "id": "wd-q17116414-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17116414-s2",
      "quote": "персонаж білоруської міфології, озерний дух — найстаріший рак, цар й покровитель усіх ракоподібних, а також захисник всього озерного царства. Він володіє величезними золотими клешнями та очима. Хабйор також допомагає людям знайти їх загублені в воді речі.",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Ukraina menyebutnya roh danau, udang karang tertua, raja dan pelindung semua krustasea serta penjaga seluruh kerajaan danau, bercapit dan bermata emas besar, yang juga membantu orang menemukan barang yang hilang di air.",
        "en": "The Ukrainian article calls him a lake spirit, the oldest crayfish, king and patron of all crustaceans and protector of the whole lake realm, with huge golden claws and eyes, who also helps people find things lost in the water."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Pyachurnik adalah roh tungku mirip kucing dalam mitologi Belarus, pelindung rumah seperti domovik yang diberi susu atau sisa makan malam.",
    "en": "Pyachurnik is a cat-like stove spirit of Belarusian mythology, a house guardian like the domovik, offered milk or supper leftovers.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q17167092-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Pyachurnik adalah roh yang tinggal di dalam atau di belakang tungku. Rupanya mirip kucing tetapi berjalan dengan kaki belakang, dan ia berusaha tidak menampakkan diri. Seperti domovik, ia melindungi rumah tempat tinggalnya, dan nyonya rumah menaruh sisa makan malam atau susu di atas tungku untuknya pada malam hari. Bila diabaikan, ia melolong dan berteriak di cerobong selama beberapa malam. Padanannya di Ukraina, Pichnyk, juga dianggap penjaga perapian rumah.",
      "en": "In Belarusian mythology Pyachurnik is a spirit living in or behind the stove. It looks like a cat but walks on its hind legs and avoids being seen. Like the domovik it guards the house where it lives, and the housewife left supper leftovers or milk on the stove for it at night. If neglected, it howls and shouts in the chimney for several nights. Its Ukrainian counterpart, the Pichnyk, is likewise a guardian of the household hearth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q17167092-c01",
        "wd-q17167092-c02",
        "wd-q17167092-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "wd-q17167092-s2",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q17167092-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q17167092-s1",
      "quote": "Пячурнік — у беларускай міфалогіі дух, які жыве ў печы ці за печчу. Абліччам нагадвае ката, толькі ходзіць на задніх лапах, чалавеку стараецца не паказвацца. З’яўляўся, як і дамавік, апекуном таго дома, дзе жыў. У знак пашаны гаспадыня выстаўляла ноччу на печы пачастунак пячурніку — рэшткі вячэры альбо малако.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Pyachurnik adalah roh yang tinggal di dalam atau di belakang tungku; rupanya mirip kucing tetapi berjalan dengan kaki belakang dan berusaha tidak menampakkan diri, dan seperti domovik ia pelindung rumah tempat tinggalnya; sebagai penghormatan, nyonya rumah menaruh sisa makan malam atau susu di atas tungku pada malam hari.",
        "en": "In Belarusian mythology Pyachurnik is a spirit living in or behind the stove; it looks like a cat but walks on its hind legs and avoids being seen, and like the domovik it is the guardian of the house it lives in; as a mark of respect the housewife set out supper leftovers or milk on the stove at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17167092-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q17167092-s2",
      "quote": "Пічник (як і домовик) — охоронець домашнього вогнища. Він постійно перебуває в печі, на печі чи за нею.",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam artikel Ukraina, Pichnyk (seperti domovyk) adalah penjaga perapian rumah yang selalu berada di dalam, di atas, atau di belakang tungku.",
        "en": "In the Ukrainian article, the Pichnyk (like the domovyk) is the guardian of the household hearth, always in, on or behind the stove."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q17167092-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q17167092-s1",
      "quote": "Калі ж да яго паставяцца без належнай увагі, пячурнік ад суму, помсты ці крыўды будзе некалькі начэй запар выць і крычаць у коміне, грукатаць уюшкаю.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila tidak diperlakukan dengan perhatian semestinya, pyachurnik karena sedih, dendam, atau tersinggung akan melolong dan berteriak di cerobong serta menggerakkan penutup cerobong dengan berisik selama beberapa malam berturut-turut.",
        "en": "If not treated with due attention, the pyachurnik, from sorrow, revenge or offence, will howl and shout in the chimney and rattle the damper for several nights in a row."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Shulikuny adalah roh najis musiman dalam kepercayaan Rusia dan Ukraina yang muncul pada masa Natal hingga Epifani dengan bara dan kait besi membara.",
    "en": "The shulikuny are seasonal unclean spirits of Russian and Ukrainian belief who appear from Christmas to Epiphany with hot coals and red-hot iron hooks.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q18407856-c01",
      "wd-q18407856-c02",
      "wd-q18407856-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bagi penduduk Rusia di utara Rusia Eropa, Ural, Siberia, dan Timur Jauh, shulikuny adalah makhluk najis masa Sviatki: roh musiman yang keluar dari air menjelang Natal atau Tahun Baru dan kembali ke sungai setelah Epifani. Menurut mitologi Ukraina, mereka lahir pada Malam Natal di cerobong tungku. Di Vologda dipercaya bahwa bayi yang dikutuk atau dibunuh ibunya menjadi shulikuny.",
      "en": "For the Russian population of northern European Russia, the Urals, Siberia and the Far East, the shulikuny are the unclean beings of Sviatki: seasonal spirits emerging from the water before Christmas or New Year and returning to the rivers after Epiphany. In Ukrainian mythology they are born on Christmas Eve in stove chimneys. In Vologda, babies cursed or killed by their mothers were believed to become shulikuny.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q18407856-c01",
        "wd-q18407856-c04",
        "wd-q18407856-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka sering sebesar kepalan tangan, bisa berkaki kuda dan berkepala runcing, dan dari mulutnya menyala api. Mereka berlarian di jalan membawa bara di wajan besi atau kait besi membara untuk menangkap orang, terutama lelaki pemabuk yang bisa mereka dorong ke lumpur atau lubang es, atau menunggang kuda, troika, lesung, dan tungku.",
      "en": "They are often fist-sized, may have horse legs and pointed heads, and fire blazes from their mouths. They run about the streets with coals in iron pans or red-hot iron hooks to catch people, especially drunken men whom they push into the mud or an ice-hole, or ride horses, troikas, mortars and stoves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q18407856-c02",
        "wd-q18407856-c03",
        "wd-q18407856-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q18407856-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q18407856-s1",
      "quote": "— у русского населения севера Европейской части России, Прикамья, Урала, Сибири, Дальнего Востока название святочной нечисти — сезонных духов, которые накануне Рождества или Нового года выходят из воды на землю, а после Крещения уходят обратно в реки и проруби.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada penduduk Rusia di utara Rusia Eropa, Prikamye, Ural, Siberia, dan Timur Jauh, shulikuny adalah nama makhluk najis masa Sviatki: roh musiman yang menjelang Natal atau Tahun Baru keluar dari air ke darat dan setelah Hari Epifani kembali ke sungai dan lubang es.",
        "en": "Among the Russian population of the north of European Russia, the Kama region, the Urals, Siberia and the Far East, shulikuny is the name of the unclean beings of Sviatki: seasonal spirits that come out of the water onto land before Christmas or New Year and go back into rivers and ice-holes after Epiphany."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q18407856-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q18407856-s1",
      "quote": "Согласно поверьям, шуликуны бегают по улицам с горячими углями на железной сковородке или железным калёным крюком в руках, которым они могут захватить людей («закрючить и сжечь»), либо ездят на конях, на тройках, на ступах или «калёных» печах.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan, shulikuny berlarian di jalan membawa bara panas di wajan besi atau kait besi membara untuk menangkap orang (\"mengait dan membakar\"), atau menunggang kuda, kereta troika, lesung, atau tungku yang membara.",
        "en": "According to belief, shulikuny run through the streets with hot coals in an iron pan or a red-hot iron hook with which they can catch people (\"hook and burn\"), or ride horses, troikas, mortars or \"red-hot\" stoves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q18407856-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q18407856-s1",
      "quote": "Ростом они нередко с кулачок, иногда побольше, могут иметь конские ноги и заострённую голову (ср. Чёрт), изо рта у них пылает огонь",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka sering sebesar kepalan tangan, kadang lebih besar, bisa berkaki kuda dan berkepala runcing (bandingkan setan), dan dari mulut mereka menyala api.",
        "en": "They are often fist-sized, sometimes larger, may have horse legs and a pointed head (compare the devil), and fire blazes from their mouths."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q18407856-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q18407856-s1",
      "quote": "По вологодским представлениям, шуликунами становятся проклятые или погубленные матерями младенцы.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan di Vologda, bayi-bayi yang dikutuk atau dibunuh ibunya menjadi shulikuny.",
        "en": "In Vologda belief, babies cursed or killed by their mothers become shulikuny."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q18407856-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q18407856-s2",
      "quote": "Шулікуни — міфічні істоти, персонажі української міфології. Ці істоти народжуються на Святвечір в коминах печей і йдуть під воду на Водохреща.",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Ukraina, shulikuny adalah makhluk mitos yang lahir pada Malam Natal di cerobong tungku dan masuk ke bawah air pada Hari Epifani.",
        "en": "In Ukrainian mythology the shulikuny are mythical beings born on Christmas Eve in stove chimneys who go under the water at Epiphany."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q18407856-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q18407856-s2",
      "quote": "якою можуть захопити чоловіка-п’яницю: крутять його, штовхають у багнюку, можуть навіть в проруб заманити.",
      "locator": "Заняття та зовнішність",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dengan kait membara itu mereka bisa menangkap lelaki pemabuk, memutar-mutarnya, mendorongnya ke lumpur, bahkan memikatnya ke lubang es.",
        "en": "With the hot hook they can catch a drunken man, spin him round, push him into the mud, even lure him into an ice-hole."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {

        "id": "Asal-usul shulikuny",

    "en": "The loyma is a female forest and marsh spirit of Belarusian mythology, described either as a frightful ugly woman or as a beautiful seductive girl.",
    "claim_ids": [

    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, loyma adalah roh perempuan yang tinggal di hutan dan rawa dan berbahaya bagi manusia. Menurut M. Federowski, loyma adalah roh jahat berambut panjang kusut dan berupa mengerikan yang menakuti perempuan desa di rawa. Artikel Ukraina menggambarkannya sebagai perempuan bertubuh hijau berkaki sangat besar yang mengganggu gadis muda dan menukar bayi dengan anaknya sendiri.",
      "en": "In Belarusian mythology the loyma is a female spirit of forests and marshes, dangerous to people. According to M. Federowski, loymas are evil spirits with long tangled hair and a frightful look who scare village women in the marshes. The Ukrainian article describes her as a green-bodied woman with huge feet who pesters young girls and swaps babies for her own offspring.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "wd-q31817365-c05",
        "wd-q31817365-c06"

      "id": "Sebaliknya, menurut catatan Mikola Haiduk, loyma adalah gadis-gadis sangat cantik yang menemui dan membelai lelaki di hutan, dan hanya menampakkan diri kepada para pembakar arang di kedalaman hutan.",
      "en": "By contrast, Mikola Haiduk's records describe loymas as very beautiful girls who meet and caress men in the forest, appearing only to charcoal-burners deep in the woods.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q31817365-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q31817365-s1",
      "quote": "у беларускай міфалогіі дух у прыгожым дзявочым абліччы. Лоймы жывуць у лясах і балотах і прадстаўляюць пагрозу для людзей.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, loyma adalah roh berwujud gadis cantik; loyma tinggal di hutan dan rawa dan berbahaya bagi manusia.",
        "en": "In Belarusian mythology the loyma is a spirit in the form of a beautiful girl; loymas live in forests and marshes and are a threat to people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q31817365-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q31817365-s1",
      "quote": "У кнізе «Люд беларускі на Русі Літоўскай» М. Федароўскі піша, што лоймы — злыя духі ў жаночым абліччы. Жывуць найчасцей у балотах і страшаць вясковых жанчын, хаця часам пужаюць і мужыкоў. У лоймаў доўгія раскудлачаныя валасы, а самі яны страшныя.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam buku \"Lud białoruski na Rusi Litewskiej\", M. Federowski menulis bahwa loyma adalah roh jahat berwujud perempuan yang paling sering tinggal di rawa dan menakuti perempuan desa, kadang juga lelaki; rambut mereka panjang dan kusut dan rupa mereka mengerikan.",
        "en": "In the book \"Lud białoruski na Rusi Litewskiej\" M. Federowski writes that loymas are evil spirits in female form, living mostly in marshes and frightening village women, sometimes men too; they have long tangled hair and are frightful."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q31817365-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q31817365-s1",
      "quote": "Паводле запісаў Міколы Гайдука, лоймы, насупраць, вельмі прыгожыя дзяўчыны. Яны спаткаюць у лесе смяротных мужчын, лашчаць іх",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebaliknya, menurut catatan Mikola Haiduk, loyma adalah gadis-gadis yang sangat cantik yang menemui lelaki fana di hutan dan membelai mereka.",
        "en": "By contrast, according to Mikola Haiduk's records, loymas are very beautiful girls who meet mortal men in the forest and caress them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q31817365-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q31817365-s1",
      "quote": "Сустракаліся яны толькі тым, хто выпальваў вугаль у лясных нетрах.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka hanya menampakkan diri kepada orang yang membakar arang di kedalaman hutan.",
        "en": "They appeared only to those who burned charcoal deep in the forest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q31817365-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q31817365-s2",
      "quote": "Найчастіше постає у вигляді огидної жінки середнього зросту, з зеленим тілом, з довгим розплатаним волоссям, звичайними руками, але з неймовірно великими ногами.",
      "locator": "Опис",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel Ukraina, ia paling sering tampil sebagai perempuan buruk rupa bertinggi sedang, bertubuh hijau, berambut panjang kusut, bertangan biasa tetapi berkaki luar biasa besar.",
        "en": "According to the Ukrainian article, she most often appears as an ugly woman of medium height with a green body, long tangled hair, ordinary hands but enormous feet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q31817365-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q31817365-s2",
      "quote": "В основному чіпляється до молодих дівчат, а також викрадає дітей у матерів, підкидаючи замість них своїх виродків",
      "locator": "Опис",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia terutama mengganggu gadis muda dan juga mencuri anak dari ibunya, menggantinya dengan anaknya sendiri yang cacat.",
        "en": "She mostly pesters young girls and also steals children from their mothers, leaving her own misshapen offspring in their place."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kudava adalah dewi pelindung rumah dalam kepercayaan pra-Kristen orang Mordvin, yang tinggal di bawah tungku, ambang pintu, atau sudut depan rumah.",
    "en": "Kudava is the house-protecting goddess of pre-Christian Mordvin belief, dwelling under the stove, the threshold or in the front corner of the house.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q32361104-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kudava adalah dewi pra-Kristen orang Mordvin, pelindung rumah. Namanya terbentuk dari kata kudo atau kud (\"rumah\") dan ava (\"perempuan\"). Ia dipercaya tinggal di bawah tungku, ambang pintu, ruang bawah lantai, atau sudut depan rumah. Kemudian orang Moksha dan sebagian Erzya menyebut dewi rumah ini Yurtava, dari kata Turkik \"yurt\".",
      "en": "Kudava is a pre-Christian Mordvin goddess, protectress of the house. Her name is formed from kudo or kud (\"house\") and ava (\"woman\"). She was believed to live under the stove, the threshold, beneath the floor or in the front corner. Later the Moksha and some Erzya called the house deity Yurtava, from the Turkic word \"yurt\".",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q32361104-c01",
        "wd-q32361104-c02",
        "wd-q32361104-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Saat membangun rumah, bulu kuda, sapi, domba, atau kambing diletakkan di bawah balok pertama agar rumah awet, dan meja disiapkan dengan roti, garam, dan anggur. Kebiasaan meletakkan bulu hewan dan uang di fondasi ini menunjukkan bahwa dahulu ada kurban nyata bagi dewi rumah.",
      "en": "When building a house, horse, cow, sheep or goat hair was laid under the first logs so the house would last, and a table was set with bread, salt and wine. This custom of putting animal hair and money in the foundation shows that real sacrifices were once made to the house deity.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q32361104-c03",
        "wd-q32361104-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Pembangunan rumah disertai persembahan bagi Kudava, yang menurut sumber dahulu berupa kurban nyata.",
    "en": "House-building was accompanied by offerings to Kudava, which the source says were once real sacrifices.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q32361104-c03",
      "wd-q32361104-c04"
    ]
  },

      ]
    },

      "en": "She comes from pre-Christian Mordvin belief.",

    "en": "Animal hair was laid under a house's first logs so it would last.",

      "id": "wd-q32361104-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q32361104-s1",
      "quote": "Кудава (м. куд, э. кудо «дом», м., э. ава «женщина») — дохристианское божество мордвы, покровительница дома. Считали, что Кудава обитает под печью, порогом, в подполье, переднем углу дома.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kudava (Moksha kud, Erzya kudo \"rumah\"; ava \"perempuan\") adalah dewi pra-Kristen orang Mordvin, pelindung rumah, yang dipercaya tinggal di bawah tungku, ambang pintu, ruang bawah lantai, atau sudut depan rumah.",
        "en": "Kudava (Moksha kud, Erzya kudo \"house\"; ava \"woman\") is a pre-Christian Mordvin deity, protectress of the house, believed to live under the stove, the threshold, beneath the floor or in the front corner of the house."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q32361104-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q32361104-s1",
      "quote": "Позднее для обозначения дома со всеми надворными постройками мокша и часть эрзи стали применять тюркское слово «юрт» и божество дома названное Юртавой.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kemudian orang Moksha dan sebagian Erzya memakai kata Turkik \"yurt\" untuk rumah beserta bangunan pekarangannya, dan dewi rumah itu disebut Yurtava.",
        "en": "Later the Moksha and some Erzya used the Turkic word \"yurt\" for the house with all its outbuildings, and the house deity was called Yurtava."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q32361104-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q32361104-s1",
      "quote": "При строительстве жилища под первые брёвна на столбах, вкапываемых в землю, клали шерсть лошади, коровы, овцы или козы, чтобы дом был долговечным. При этом накрывали стол, ставили на него хлеб, соль, вино.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat membangun rumah, bulu kuda, sapi, domba, atau kambing diletakkan di bawah balok pertama di atas tiang yang ditanam agar rumah awet, dan meja disiapkan dengan roti, garam, dan anggur.",
        "en": "When building a house, horse, cow, sheep or goat hair was laid under the first logs on the posts sunk into the ground so the house would last, and a table was set with bread, salt and wine."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q32361104-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q32361104-s1",
      "quote": "Обычай класть в основание дома шерсть животного и деньги говорит о том, что в старину были не символические, а реальные жертвоприношения божеству дома.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kebiasaan meletakkan bulu hewan dan uang di fondasi rumah menunjukkan bahwa dahulu ada kurban nyata, bukan sekadar simbolis, bagi dewi rumah.",
        "en": "The custom of putting animal hair and money in a house's foundation shows that in the past there were real, not symbolic, sacrifices to the house deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q32361104-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q32361104-s2",
      "quote": "Кудава — кудонь ванстыця. Кудава валось теевсь кавто валтнэнь эйстэ — «кудо» ды «ава».",
      "locator": "вводка",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Erzya: Kudava adalah penjaga rumah, dan kata Kudava terbentuk dari dua kata, \"kudo\" dan \"ava\".",
        "en": "In Erzya: Kudava is the guardian of the house, and the word Kudava is formed from two words, \"kudo\" and \"ava\"."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "culture",
      "searched": "Tidak ada ID budaya Mordvin di §8.2; dipakai tradition-finno-ugric."
    },
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Artikel ru bertanda kurang catatan kaki; Ensiklopedia Mordovia (Mokshin, Shulyaev) yang dirujuk belum dibuka. Artikel myv hanya dua kalimat."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dana adalah dewi air hipotetis dalam mitologi Slavia yang diusulkan N. I. Kostomarov; ilmu modern menolak hipotesis ini, meski nama Dana muncul dalam lagu dan cerita rakyat.",
    "en": "Dana is a hypothetical water goddess of Slavic mythology proposed by N. I. Kostomarov; modern scholarship rejects the hypothesis, though the name Dana appears in folk songs and tales.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q4154464-c01",
      "wd-q4154464-c02",
      "wd-q4154464-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Menurut N. I. Kostomarov, Dana adalah dewi air dalam mitologi Slavia, pelindung sungai, anak sungai, dan perairan. Ia mendasarkan pendapatnya pada nama sungai seperti Dvina, Danube, Don, dan Dnieper, serta refrein \"dana, dana\" dalam lagu ritual, dan menggambarkan Dana sebagai perawan sekaligus istri, permaisuri matahari. Ilmu modern menganggap hipotesis ini keliru.",
      "en": "According to N. I. Kostomarov, Dana is a Slavic water goddess, patroness of rivers, streams and waters. He based this on river names such as the Dvina, Danube, Don and Dnieper and the refrain \"dana, dana\" in ritual songs, and pictured Dana as both maiden and wife, consort of the sun. Modern scholarship considers the hypothesis mistaken.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q4154464-c01",
        "wd-q4154464-c02",
        "wd-q4154464-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Artikel Ukraina menyebut Dana dewi air hipotetis yang mungkin juga bernama Divanna, Divoniya, atau Diva. Danylo Lepky mencatat bahwa semua rusalka mematuhi seorang nyonya tertua yang oleh rakyat disebut Diva, Divka, Divchyna, atau Dana.",
      "en": "The Ukrainian article calls Dana a hypothetical water goddess, possibly also named Divanna, Divoniya or Diva. Danylo Lepky recorded that all the rusalkas obey an eldest lady whom the people call Diva, Divka, Divchyna or Dana.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q4154464-c04",
        "wd-q4154464-c05"

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q4154464-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q4154464-s1",
      "quote": "— по мнению Н. И. Костомарова, богиня воды в славянской мифологии, покровительница рек, ручьев и водоёмов. Современной наукой гипотеза признана ошибочной.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut N. I. Kostomarov, Dana adalah dewi air dalam mitologi Slavia, pelindung sungai, anak sungai, dan perairan; ilmu modern menganggap hipotesis ini keliru.",
        "en": "According to N. I. Kostomarov, Dana is a water goddess of Slavic mythology, patroness of rivers, streams and waters; modern scholarship considers the hypothesis mistaken."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4154464-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q4154464-s1",
      "quote": "Свой вывод он аргументирует, приводя названия рек: Двина, Дунай, Дон, Днепр (Данаприс) и припев в обрядных песнях: дана, дана.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kostomarov mendasarkan kesimpulannya pada nama-nama sungai, seperti Dvina, Danube (Dunai), Don, dan Dnieper (Danapris), serta refrein \"dana, dana\" dalam lagu-lagu ritual.",
        "en": "Kostomarov supported his conclusion with river names such as the Dvina, Danube (Dunai), Don and Dnieper (Danapris) and the refrain \"dana, dana\" in ritual songs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4154464-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q4154464-s1",
      "quote": "О Дане Костомаров пишет: «как вода, начало вещей, вечно прекрасная, вечно свежая, она была дева и вместе жена, то есть супруга солнца».",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kostomarov menulis tentang Dana: \"seperti air, awal segala sesuatu, abadi jelita, abadi segar, ia adalah perawan sekaligus istri, yaitu permaisuri matahari.\"",
        "en": "Kostomarov wrote of Dana: \"like water, the beginning of things, eternally beautiful, eternally fresh, she was a maiden and at the same time a wife, that is, the consort of the sun.\""
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4154464-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q4154464-s2",
      "quote": "— гіпотетична богиня води в слов'янській міфології, покровителька річок, струмків і водойм. Також її іменами можуть бути Діванна, Дівонія, Діва.",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Ukraina menyebut Dana dewi air hipotetis dalam mitologi Slavia, pelindung sungai, anak sungai, dan perairan, yang mungkin juga bernama Divanna, Divoniya, atau Diva.",
        "en": "The Ukrainian article calls Dana a hypothetical water goddess of Slavic mythology, patroness of rivers, streams and waters, possibly also named Divanna, Divoniya or Diva."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q4154464-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q4154464-s2",
      "quote": "Данило Лепкий у розвідці «Весняні звичаї, обряди та віруваня на Руси» писав ... Всї Русалки слухають одної найстаршої панї, котру простий люд в своїх оповіданях називає просто «Діва», «Дівка», «Дівчина» або «Дана».",
      "locator": "У народних віруваннях",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Danylo Lepky dalam kajian tentang adat dan kepercayaan musim semi di Rus menulis bahwa semua rusalka mematuhi seorang nyonya tertua, yang oleh rakyat dalam cerita-cerita mereka disebut \"Diva\", \"Divka\", \"Divchyna\", atau \"Dana\".",
        "en": "Danylo Lepky, in his study of spring customs and beliefs in Rus, wrote that all the rusalkas obey one eldest lady, whom the common people in their tales simply call \"Diva\", \"Divka\", \"Divchyna\" or \"Dana\"."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Zhaleznaya baba adalah roh jahat dalam mitologi Belarus, nenek berpayudara besi yang dipakai menakut-nakuti anak agar tidak pergi ke kebun, ladang, atau sumur.",
    "en": "Zhaleznaya baba is an evil spirit of Belarusian mythology, an iron-breasted hag used to scare children away from gardens, fields and wells.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q45112940-c01",
      "wd-q45112940-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Zhaleznaya baba adalah roh jahat yang dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak-anak. Ia tinggal di kebun atau ladang, berupa nenek tua pendek berpayudara besi yang menangkap anak-anak yang pergi tanpa izin orang tua dengan kait besi, menumbuk mereka di lesung besinya, lalu memakannya. Menurut etnograf Shakhovich, di daerah lain ia dipercaya tinggal di sumur dan menarik anak-anak yang mengintip ke dalamnya. Ia mirip Rusalka dan Baba Yaga.",
      "en": "In Belarusian mythology Zhaleznaya baba is an evil spirit used to frighten children. She lives in the garden or field, a short old woman with iron breasts who catches children who wander off without permission with an iron hook, pounds them in her iron mortar and eats them. According to the ethnographer Shakhovich, elsewhere she was believed to live in a well and pull in children who peered in. She resembles the Rusalka and Baba Yaga.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q45112940-c01",
        "wd-q45112940-c02",
        "wd-q45112940-c03",
        "wd-q45112940-c04"

    {
      "id": "wd-q45112940-s2",
      "url": "https://uk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%97%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%96%D0%B7%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%B1%D0%B0%D0%B1%D0%B0",
      "title": "Залізна баба",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (uk)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "uk",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q45112940-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q45112940-s1",
      "quote": "Жалезная баба - у беларускай міфалогіі злы дух, якім пужалі дзяцей.",
      "locator": "уводзіны",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Belarus, Zhaleznaya baba (Nenek Besi) adalah roh jahat yang dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak-anak.",
        "en": "In Belarusian mythology Zhaleznaya baba (Iron Hag) is an evil spirit used to frighten children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q45112940-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q45112940-s1",
      "quote": "Жыве ў агародзе ці ў полі. Злая старая бабка нізенькага росту, з жалезнымі грудзямі. Дзяцей, якія без дазволу бацькоў ходзяць у агарод ці ў поле, яна хапае жалезным круком, кідае ў сваю жалезную ступу, таўчэ там, а потым з'ядае.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tinggal di kebun atau ladang, berupa nenek tua jahat bertubuh pendek dengan payudara besi; anak-anak yang pergi ke kebun atau ladang tanpa izin orang tua ia tangkap dengan kait besi, dilempar ke lesung besinya, ditumbuk, lalu dimakan.",
        "en": "She lives in the garden or field, a wicked short old woman with iron breasts; children who go into the garden or field without their parents' permission she catches with an iron hook, throws into her iron mortar, pounds and then eats."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q45112940-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q45112940-s1",
      "quote": "Паводле звестак этнографа Шаховіча, у іншых мясцінах Беларусі верылі, быццам Жалезная баба жыве ў студні. Дзяцей, якія заглядаюць у студню, яна ўцягвае да сябе.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut etnograf Shakhovich, di daerah lain di Belarus orang percaya Zhaleznaya baba tinggal di sumur dan menarik masuk anak-anak yang mengintip ke dalamnya.",
        "en": "According to the ethnographer Shakhovich, in other parts of Belarus she was believed to live in a well and to pull in children who peered into it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q45112940-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q45112940-s2",
      "quote": "Вона, також, має певну схожість з Русалкою та Бабоюю-Ягою.",
      "locator": "Опис",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Ukraina menyebut bahwa ia memiliki kemiripan tertentu dengan Rusalka dan Baba Yaga.",
        "en": "The Ukrainian article notes that she bears some resemblance to the Rusalka and Baba Yaga."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

    "en": "Afruvvá is a mermaid of coastal Sami tradition in Finnmark, originally a guardian spirit warning seafarers to return to shore in time.",

    {
      "id": "Afruvvá, dari bahasa Norwegia havs fru (\"nyonya laut\"), adalah makhluk yang terutama dikenal di kalangan Sami pesisir di pantai Finnmark, bagian dari tradisi cerita yang relatif muda dan dimiliki bersama orang Skandinavia. Ia putri duyung khas yang cantik, berambut panjang, dan berekor ikan. Awalnya ia tampaknya roh pelindung yang memperingatkan pelaut agar pulang tepat waktu; versi yang membalikkan perahu mungkin mengambil ciri rávgá, arwah orang tenggelam.",
      "en": "Afruvvá, from Norwegian havs fru (\"lady of the sea\"), is a being known mainly among the coastal Sami of the Finnmark coast, part of a relatively young storytelling tradition shared with the Scandinavians. She is a typical beautiful, long-haired, fish-tailed mermaid. Originally she seems to have been a guardian spirit warning seafarers to return home in time; the boat-capsizing version may have borrowed traits of the rávgá, a spirit of the drowned dead.",

      "id": "afruvva-c01",
      "source_id": "afruvva-s1",
      "quote": "Afruvvá (< norjan havs fru, 'meren rouva') on lähinnä Ruijan rannikon merisaamelaisten parissa tunnettu olento, joka edustaa saamelaisten parissa nuorta, skandinaavien kanssa yhteistä kertomusperinnettä.",
      "locator": "johdanto",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Afruvvá (dari bahasa Norwegia havs fru, \"nyonya laut\") adalah makhluk yang terutama dikenal di kalangan Sami pesisir di pantai Finnmark (Ruija), mewakili tradisi cerita yang relatif muda dan dimiliki bersama orang Skandinavia.",
        "en": "Afruvvá (from Norwegian havs fru, \"lady of the sea\") is a being known mainly among the coastal Sami of the Finnmark (Ruija) coast, representing a relatively young storytelling tradition shared with the Scandinavians."

    },
    {
      "id": "afruvva-c02",
      "source_id": "afruvva-s1",
      "quote": "Afruvvá on tyypillinen merenneito, kaunis, pitkätukkainen ja kalanpyrstöinen nainen, joka näyttäytyy merellä.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Afruvvá adalah putri duyung khas: perempuan cantik berambut panjang dan berekor ikan yang menampakkan diri di laut.",
        "en": "Afruvvá is a typical mermaid, a beautiful long-haired, fish-tailed woman who appears at sea."
      }

    {
      "id": "afruvva-c03",
      "source_id": "afruvva-s1",
      "quote": "Alun perin afruvvá lienee esiintynyt merenkävijöitä suojelevana haltijana, joka näille näyttäytymällä kehottaa heitä palaamaan ajoissa rantaan.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Awalnya afruvvá tampaknya roh pelindung pelaut yang, dengan menampakkan diri, mendesak mereka kembali ke pantai tepat waktu.",
        "en": "Originally afruvvá was probably a guardian spirit of seafarers who, by appearing to them, urged them to return to shore in time."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "afruvva-c04",
      "source_id": "afruvva-s1",
      "quote": "Toisinaan hänen kylläkin mainitaan myös kaatavan veneitä ja tuottavan muuta häiriötä, mutta tämä merenkävijöitä ahdisteleva avruvvá lienee saanut toisen skandinaavisperäisen vedenhaltijan, vainajaolentoihin luokiteltavan rávgán piirteitä.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia juga disebut membalikkan perahu dan menimbulkan gangguan lain, tetapi afruvvá pengganggu pelaut ini tampaknya mengambil ciri roh air asal Skandinavia lain, yaitu rávgá yang tergolong arwah orang mati.",
        "en": "Sometimes she is also said to capsize boats and cause other trouble, but this seafarer-harassing afruvvá has probably taken on traits of another water spirit of Scandinavian origin, the rávgá, classed among the dead."
      }

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Anki-Kiele adalah roh laut dalam mitologi Chukchi dan Eskimo, yang tinggal di dasar laut dan dapat memberi tangkapan baik bila didatangi jiwa seorang dukun.",
    "en": "Anki-Kiele is the sea spirit of Chukchi and Eskimo mythology, living on the sea bed and able to grant a good catch when a shaman's soul visits him.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "anki-kiele-c01",
      "anki-kiele-c03",
      "anki-kiele-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Anki-Kiele, juga Anky-Kele atau Anka-Kal, adalah roh laut dalam mitologi Chukchi dan Eskimo. Ia termasuk roh kele, golongan yang mencakup kekuatan yang memusuhi manusia maupun roh penguasa. Menurut gambar seniman Chukchi Onno, ia tinggal di dasar laut dan berkepala besar berbulu dengan mulut lebar bergigi, sedangkan gambar dari koleksi V. G. Bogoraz menggambarkannya bertubuh ikan. Ia bisa memberi tangkapan ikan atau buruan laut yang baik, yang diupayakan dengan mengirim jiwa seorang dukun kepadanya.",
      "en": "Anki-Kiele, also Anky-Kele or Anka-Kal, is the sea spirit of Chukchi and Eskimo mythology. He belongs to the kele spirits, a category embracing both forces hostile to people and master spirits. According to a drawing by the Chukchi artist Onno he lives on the sea bed and has a huge shaggy head with a large toothy mouth, while a drawing from V. G. Bogoraz's collection gives him a fish body. He could grant a good catch of fish or sea game, sought by sending a shaman's soul to him.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anki-kiele-c01",
        "anki-kiele-c02",
        "anki-kiele-c03",
        "anki-kiele-c04",
        "anki-kiele-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "source_id": "anki-kiele-s1",
      "quote": "(также Анкы-Келе, Анка-Кал; чук. «дух моря») — дух моря в чукотской и эскимосской мифологии.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anki-Kiele (juga Anky-Kele, Anka-Kal; bahasa Chukchi: \"roh laut\") adalah roh laut dalam mitologi Chukchi dan Eskimo.",
        "en": "Anki-Kiele (also Anky-Kele, Anka-Kal; Chukchi \"spirit of the sea\") is the sea spirit of Chukchi and Eskimo mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anki-kiele-c02",
      "source_id": "anki-kiele-s1",
      "quote": "Анки-Киеле относится к категории духов-келе, которая включает в себя не только враждебные человеку силы, но и духов-хозяев (аун-ральыт).",
      "locator": "Описание и основные функции",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anki-Kiele termasuk golongan roh kele, yang mencakup bukan hanya kekuatan yang memusuhi manusia, tetapi juga roh-roh penguasa (aun-ralyt).",
        "en": "Anki-Kiele belongs to the kele spirits, a category including not only forces hostile to people but also master spirits (aun-ralyt)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anki-kiele-c03",
      "source_id": "anki-kiele-s1",
      "quote": "Согласно рисунку чукчи Онно (1912—1953), Анки-Киеле живёт на дне моря, имеет огромную мохнатую голову с двумя глазами и большой зубастой пастью.",
      "locator": "Описание и основные функции",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut gambar seniman Chukchi Onno (1912–1953), Anki-Kiele tinggal di dasar laut dan berkepala besar berbulu dengan dua mata dan mulut lebar bergigi.",
        "en": "According to a drawing by the Chukchi artist Onno (1912–1953), Anki-Kiele lives on the sea bed and has a huge shaggy head with two eyes and a large toothy maw."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anki-kiele-c04",
      "source_id": "anki-kiele-s1",
      "quote": "Рисунок из собрания В. Г. Богораза ... изображает Анки-Киеле с телом рыбы.",
      "locator": "Описание и основные функции",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah gambar dari koleksi V. G. Bogoraz menggambarkan Anki-Kiele bertubuh ikan.",
        "en": "A drawing from V. G. Bogoraz's collection shows Anki-Kiele with the body of a fish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anki-kiele-c05",
      "source_id": "anki-kiele-s1",
      "quote": "Анки-Киеле мог послать хороший улов рыбы или успех в добыче прочих морских обитателей. Этого можно было добиться, послав к нему душу шамана.",
      "locator": "Описание и основные функции",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anki-Kiele bisa memberikan tangkapan ikan yang baik atau keberhasilan berburu hewan laut lain, yang bisa diupayakan dengan mengirim jiwa seorang dukun (shaman) kepadanya.",
        "en": "Anki-Kiele could grant a good catch of fish or success in hunting other sea creatures, which could be obtained by sending a shaman's soul to him."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Babaroga adalah nenek bertanduk dalam mitologi Slavia Selatan yang keluar malam hari dan menculik anak-anak nakal.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Babaroga adalah makhluk dari mitologi Slavia Selatan yang dulu dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak-anak. Ia digambarkan sebagai nenek bungkuk, buruk rupa, dan ompong dengan tanduk di kepala, yang menjadi asal namanya. Ia bersembunyi di tempat gelap, keluar hanya malam hari, dan menculik anak-anak nakal ke sarangnya. Ia sering dicampuradukkan dengan penyihir, padahal keduanya makhluk yang berbeda.",
      "en": "Babaroga is a being of South Slavic mythology once used to frighten children. She is pictured as a hunched, ugly, toothless old woman with a horn on her head, which gives her her name. She hides in dark places, comes out only at night and carries naughty children off to her lair. She is often confused with the witch, though they are different beings.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Božalość adalah pembawa kabar kematian dalam mitologi Wend (Sorbia Lusatia), perempuan berpakaian putih yang menangis di bawah jendela orang yang akan meninggal.",
    "en": "Božalość is a messenger of death in the mythology of the Wends (Lusatian Sorbs), a woman in white who weeps under the window of someone about to die.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bozalosc-c01",
      "bozalosc-c03",
      "bozalosc-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Božalość adalah pembawa kabar kemalangan atau kematian dalam mitologi Wend, yaitu orang Sorbia Lusatia. Sumber etnografi Jerman menerjemahkan namanya sebagai \"Gottesklage\", Ratapan Tuhan. Artikel W. v. Schulenburg tahun 1886 mengaitkannya dengan semak elder dan menggambarkannya sebagai perempuan berpakaian putih berambut panjang kusut dan bermata merah, sedangkan Biren Bonnerjea menggambarkannya sebagai perempuan kecil yang menangis di bawah jendela orang yang akan meninggal.",
      "en": "Božalość is a messenger of misfortune or death in the mythology of the Wends, the Lusatian Sorbs. German ethnographic sources rendered the name as \"Gottesklage\", God's Lament. An 1886 article by W. v. Schulenburg links it with the elder bush and describes a woman in white with long unkempt hair and red eyes, while Biren Bonnerjea describes a little woman who weeps under the window of someone about to die.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bozalosc-c01",
        "bozalosc-c02",
        "bozalosc-c03",
        "bozalosc-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Makhluk sejajarnya, bože sedleško, digambarkan sebagai anak berpakaian putih; penampakannya menandakan kematian, kebakaran, wabah, atau banjir yang akan datang.",
      "en": "A parallel being, the bože sedleško, is described as a child in white; its appearance foretells coming death, fire, epidemic or flood.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bozalosc-c05",
        "bozalosc-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Čáhkalakkis adalah makhluk bawah tanah seukuran anak kecil dalam tradisi cerita Sami yang tinggal di rongga gunung, baik hati, dan memiliki emas dan perak.",
    "en": "The čáhkalakkis is a child-sized underground being of Sami storytelling that lives in mountain hollows, is benevolent and owns gold and silver.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi cerita Sami, čáhkalakkis adalah makhluk bawah tanah seukuran anak kecil yang tinggal di rongga-rongga gunung; padanannya dikenal di kalangan Sami Skolt, Kildin, Ter, dan Inari dengan nama čeäk'kli, čahhkli, dan tšahalag. Ia makhluk baik hati yang tampil telanjang atau berpakaian minim, bagian dari bangsa yang asing tetapi tidak bermusuhan, dan berbicara dengan bahasanya sendiri.",
      "en": "In Sami storytelling the čáhkalakkis is a child-sized underground being living in mountain hollows; counterparts are known among the Skolt, Kildin, Ter and Inari Sami as the čeäk'kli, čahhkli and tšahalag. It is a benevolent being that appears naked or scantily dressed, one of a people foreign but not hostile, speaking its own language.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cahkalakkis-c01",
        "cahkalakkis-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Čáhkalakkis memiliki emas dan perak yang bisa didapat dengan cara tertentu: bubur dan sepatu bulu bertali diletakkan di dekat rongganya sebagai umpan. Ketika harta mereka habis, mereka mengirim sendok kayu sebagai tanda, dan orang tak boleh mengambil semuanya karena keturunan orang tamak akan jatuh miskin. Motif emas dan perak ini diduga berasal dari tradisi Skandinavia kuno atau Jermanik.",
      "en": "The čáhkalakkis owns gold and silver that can be obtained by a trick: porridge and a laced fur shoe are set by its hollow as bait. When their riches ran out they sent wooden spoons as a sign, and one must not take everything, since a greedy person's descendants become poor. The gold-and-silver motif presumably comes from Old Scandinavian or Germanic tradition.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cahkalakkis-c03",
        "cahkalakkis-c04",
        "cahkalakkis-c05",
        "cahkalakkis-c06"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "cahkalakkis-c01",
      "source_id": "cahkalakkis-s1",
      "quote": "Čáhkalakkis on saamelaisessa kertomusperinteessä maanalainen, pikkulapsen kokoinen, vuorien onkaloissa asuva olento. Sitä vastaavat koltansaamelaisilla čeäk'kli, kildinin- ja turjansaamelaisilla čahhkli ja inarinsaamelaisilla tšahalag.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi cerita Sami, čáhkalakkis adalah makhluk bawah tanah seukuran anak kecil yang tinggal di rongga-rongga gunung; padanannya pada orang Sami Skolt disebut čeäk'kli, pada Sami Kildin dan Ter čahhkli, dan pada Sami Inari tšahalag.",
        "en": "In Sami storytelling tradition the čáhkalakkis is an underground being the size of a small child living in mountain hollows; among the Skolt Sami its counterpart is the čeäk'kli, among the Kildin and Ter Sami the čahhkli, and among the Inari Sami the tšahalag."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cahkalakkis-c02",
      "source_id": "cahkalakkis-s1",
      "quote": "Čáhkalakkis on hyväntahtoinen olento, joka esiintyy joko alastomana tai vähissä vaatteissa. Nämä maahiset ovat saamelaisille vierasta, mutta eivät vihamielistä kansaa. Ne puhuvat omaa kieltään, jota saamelaiset vaivoin ymmärtävät.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Čáhkalakkis adalah makhluk baik hati yang tampil telanjang atau berpakaian minim; makhluk bawah tanah ini bangsa yang asing tetapi tidak bermusuhan bagi orang Sami, dan berbicara dengan bahasa sendiri yang sulit dipahami orang Sami.",
        "en": "The čáhkalakkis is a benevolent being that appears naked or scantily dressed; these earth-dwellers are a people foreign but not hostile to the Sami, and they speak their own language, which the Sami understand only with difficulty."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cahkalakkis-c03",
      "source_id": "cahkalakkis-s1",
      "quote": "Ne omistavat erilaisia tavaroita, kultaa ja hopeaa, joita ihminen voi saada tietyllä konstilla.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka memiliki berbagai barang, emas, dan perak, yang bisa didapat manusia dengan cara tertentu.",
        "en": "They own various goods, gold and silver, which a person can obtain by a certain trick."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cahkalakkis-c04",
      "source_id": "cahkalakkis-s1",
      "quote": "Saadakseen maahisen kiinni on asetettava onkalon viereen puuroa sekä karvakenkä pauloineen syötiksi.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk menangkap makhluk bawah tanah ini, bubur dan sepatu bulu beserta talinya harus diletakkan di dekat rongga sebagai umpan.",
        "en": "To catch the earth-dweller, porridge and a fur shoe with its laces must be set by the hollow as bait."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cahkalakkis-c05",
      "source_id": "cahkalakkis-s1",
      "quote": "Kun maahisilta loppuivat rikkaudet, ne lähettivät ihmisille puulusikat merkiksi siitä, ettei niillä ole enää mitään annettavaa. Maahisilta ei saanut ottaa kaikkea, koska ahneen ihmisen jälkeläiset köyhtyvät.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika harta mereka habis, makhluk bawah tanah itu mengirim sendok kayu kepada manusia sebagai tanda tak ada lagi yang bisa diberikan; orang tak boleh mengambil segalanya dari mereka, karena keturunan orang tamak akan jatuh miskin.",
        "en": "When the earth-dwellers ran out of riches they sent people wooden spoons as a sign that they had nothing more to give; one must not take everything from them, because a greedy person's descendants become poor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cahkalakkis-c06",
      "source_id": "cahkalakkis-s1",
      "quote": "Kulta-hopea -motiivi čáhkalakkis-perinteessä sekä haltija-aarteenomistaja -ulottuvuus ovat otaksuttavasti muinaisskandinaavista tai jopa germaanista alkuperää.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Motif emas dan perak dalam tradisi čáhkalakkis serta peran sebagai roh pemilik harta diduga berasal dari tradisi Skandinavia kuno atau bahkan Jermanik.",
        "en": "The gold-and-silver motif in the čáhkalakkis tradition and its role as a treasure-owning spirit are presumably of Old Scandinavian or even Germanic origin."
      }
    }
